# AUTEF v2 — gap analysis

Adversarial audit: 8 dimension auditors, 164 findings verified one-by-one by an independent skeptic, plus a completeness critic. **163 confirmed**, 1 refuted, 10 added by the critic. 35 of the confirmed gaps can stop a run reaching stage 9 green.

Severity counts: **blocker** 3, **major** 72, **minor** 88


## flow  (19)

### [blocker] AUTEF-authored tests that end the repair path unfixed are never removed, excised, or quarantined — and no helper exists that could remove them at the right gran
*blocks end-to-end: True · confidence 0.95*

**Evidence.** `orchestrator.py:171-225` — the repair loop ends at `self.cache.record_failure(...)` / `return record` with no action on the test file. `enhance.py:607-621` `_remove_generated` exists but is only ever called from generation/coverage *validation* (`enhance.py:328`, `337`) and mutation kill-verification (`545`, `571`) — never from the repair path. `grep -rn "xfail|quarantine" src/autef2` returns only `guards.py:110,138` and `strategies.py:39,49`, which *reject* a repair that adds skip/xfail. So there is literally no code path by which an AUTEF-authored test that the 3-rung ladder could not fix leaves the suite.

**Why it matters.** This is the mechanical cause of the user's core complaint (E1, E5). Stage 4 writes ~25 unreviewed tests; every one the ladder cannot fix is left in the project permanently red. `enhance.py:470-476` then counts exactly those tests when deciding whether stage 8 may run, so AUTEF's own unfixable output gates AUTEF's own mutation stage off. The suite the user is handed back at stage 9 is redder than the one they uploaded, and nothing in the pipeline can ever recover.

**Fix sketch.** After the ladder is exhausted, branch on provenance: if `authored_here(test_file)` (generation.py:42-49), either delete the generated test function or mark it `@pytest.mark.xfail(strict=False, reason=...)` and record it as quarantined in the report, so the green gate and the final suite reflect the project's own tests, not AUTEF's failed guesses.

**Verifier's correction.** AUTEF-authored tests that end the repair path unfixed are never removed, excised, or quarantined — and no helper exists that could remove them at the right granularity.

Three exits in `orchestrator.py:repair` strand a generated test in the project permanently red: `NON_REPAIRABLE` diagnosis before the loop (`orchestrator.py:159-165`), `source_defect_claimed` mid-loop (`204-209`), and ladder exhaustion (`222-225`). None takes any action on the test file, and `orchestrator.run()` (`80-121`) adds no post-loop cleanup. Retention is deliberate: `enhance.py:166-173` states a generated file "is kept even when its tests fail", discarding only when pytest could not run it (`176-182`) or collected nothing (`189-192`). Stage 7 leaks the same way — `CoveragePhase._validate` (`enhance.py:321-337`) checks only `ran` and `tests_collected`, never `tests_passing`.

`_remove_generated` (`enhance.py:607-621`) is called only from generation/coverage validation (`328`, `337`) and mutation kill-verification (`545`, `571`); `grep` confirms no repair-path caller. No quarantine path exists either: `guards.py:110,138` and `strategies.py:39,49` explicitly *reject* a repair that adds skip/xfail.

### [major] On the CLI path, `RunReport.before` is the suite state AFTER stage-4 generation, contradicting the semantics the code documents for it. `pipeline.py:82-91` runs
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.95*

**Evidence.** `pipeline.py:82-91` runs `GenerationPhase(...).run(...)` and writes test files, then `pipeline.py:93-94` builds `RepairOrchestrator` and calls `orchestrator.run()`, whose first act is `orchestrator.py:85-86`: `before = self.runner.run_suite(); report.before = before`. There is no earlier suite run on this path. The web path does it correctly (`server.py:264-267` stage 3 sets `before`; `server.py:269-288` stage 4 calls `_collect_suite(..., baseline=False)`), and `Session.reset_from` `server.py:150-153` explicitly comments "Stage 4 keeps ``before``" — so the two paths disagree.

**Why it matters.** On `autef2 run --all-phases` the baseline that every headline number is measured against already contains AUTEF's own generated tests. `pipeline.py:206-216` prints `before: N passed, M failed` from it, `models.py:556-560` serialises it into the JSON, and `eval/` consumes it. The v1-vs-v2 comparison the HOD asked for (E7) would therefore be computed against a baseline AUTEF itself modified. `_repair_again`'s docstring (`pipeline.py:150-154`) claims "``before`` is left alone: it describes the project as it arrived" — for the second pass only; the first pass already polluted it.

**Fix sketch.** Run the suite once in `run_project` before the generation block and pass it into `RepairOrchestrator.run` (or set `report.before` there), so `before` always describes the project as ingested on both the CLI and web paths.

**Verifier's correction.** On the CLI path, `RunReport.before` is the suite state AFTER stage-4 generation, contradicting the semantics the code documents for it. `pipeline.py:82-91` runs `GenerationPhase` (which keeps generated files even when their tests fail, `enhance.py:166-201`) before `pipeline.py:93-94` calls `RepairOrchestrator.run()`, whose first act is the baseline run at `orchestrator.py:85-86`. The web path instead takes `before` at stage 3 (`server.py:264-266`, baseline=True) and explicitly preserves it across generation (`server.py:152-153`, `server.py:287` baseline=False), matching the meaning asserted in `pipeline.py:150-154` and `server.py:201-206` ("the project as it arrived"). Consequently `autef2 run --generate/--all-phases` prints (`pipeline.py:206-211`) and serialises (`models.py:551`) a "before" that already contains AUTEF's own generated tests, and `web/report.py:394` labels such a snapshot "Stage 3 - the suite as it arrived". Scope correction: this does NOT affect the v1-vs-v2 benchmark, which never calls `run_project` and deliberately generates once before both arms from a shared pristine copy (`eval/benchmark.py:209-221`, `247-250`); and the post-generation snapshot is in fact the 

### [major] The mutation gate reason survives only as a console log line and is dropped from both persisted CLI outputs. On the `require_green` path, `enhance.py:463-480` r
*blocks end-to-end: True · confidence 0.95*

**Evidence.** `enhance.py:463-480` sets `outcome.skipped_reason` and returns with `outcome.before` still `None`. `pipeline.py:124-128` assigns `report.mutation_before = outcome.before` (None) and only does `logger.info("mutation phase skipped: %s", ...)` — there is no `mutation_skipped_reason` field on `RunReport` (`models.py:483-504`) and none in `to_dict` (`models.py:556-560`). `pipeline.py:235` guards the whole mutation block with `if report.mutation_before is not None:`, and `cli.py:149` guards its survivor listing with `if report.mutation_before and report.mutation_before.measured:`.

**Why it matters.** A user who runs `autef2 run --all-phases` and hits the green gate (E1) gets a summary and a JSON report with *no mention of mutation at all* — not the score, not the fact it was skipped, not why. The single most important explanation for "it won't reach mutation" exists only as a transient INFO log line. The web UI does surface it (`server.py:761`, `app.js:575`), so the CLI is strictly worse.

**Fix sketch.** Add `mutation_skipped_reason: Optional[str]` to `RunReport`, set it in `pipeline.py:127`, serialise it in `to_dict`, and print it in `summarise` and `cli._cmd_run` whenever `--mutation`/`--all-phases` was requested but `mutation_before` is None.

**Verifier's correction.** The mutation gate reason survives only as a console log line and is dropped from both persisted CLI outputs. On the `require_green` path, `enhance.py:463-480` returns with `outcome.before` still None; `pipeline.py:123-128` copies only before/after/records and never stores `skipped_reason`, `RunReport` has no field for it (`models.py:483-504`) and `to_dict` emits none (`models.py:559-560`). Consequently `summarise()` prints no mutation line at all (guard at `pipeline.py:235`) and the `--json` report writes `mutation_before: null, mutation_after: null, mutation_generated: []` — byte-identical to a run where `--mutation` was never requested (`pipeline.py:114`). The reason IS shown live, at WARNING level, via `enhance.py:478` under the root handler configured at `config.py:233-237` (default level INFO, `cli.py:33,279`), so the run is not silent and nothing is blocked — exit code is 0 (`cli.py:178`). The defect is that the machine-readable artifact and the copy-pasteable summary lose the explanation, and that `eval/metrics.py` and `eval/compare.py` (which only read repair-record `skipped_reason`) inherit the same blind spot, so a benchmark row cannot say why mutation was absent. ### [major] `MutationPhase.run`'s `require_green` parameter (src/autef2/enhance.py:458, used at :463) is dead configurability: neither caller passes it — pipeline.py:118-12
*blocks end-to-end: True · confidence 0.9*

**Evidence.** `grep -rn require_green src/ tests_autef2/` matches only `enhance.py:458` (`require_green: bool = True` parameter) and `enhance.py:463` (its use). Neither caller passes it: `pipeline.py:118-123` calls `phase.run(max_mutants=..., max_survivors=..., seed=...)` and `server.py:431-435` calls `.run(max_mutants=..., max_survivors=...)`. There is no `require_green` field in `AutefConfig` (`config.py:38-94`), no `--force-mutation` flag in `cli.py:295-320`, and no control in `web/static/index.html:117-186`.

**Why it matters.** Once the gate trips there is no supported way to proceed — not even for a thesis run where the operator knows the three red tests are AUTEF's own and wants a mutation number anyway. The parameter's existence implies configurability that does not exist anywhere a user can reach.

**Fix sketch.** Promote it to `AutefConfig.mutation_require_green`, wire an `--allow-red-mutation` CLI flag and a UI checkbox, and when overridden stamp the resulting `MutationSnapshot` with a caveat so the report cannot present an ungated score as a clean one.

**Verifier's correction.** `MutationPhase.run`'s `require_green` parameter (src/autef2/enhance.py:458, used at :463) is dead configurability: neither caller passes it — pipeline.py:118-122 and web/server.py:431-437 — and there is no `require_green` field in `AutefConfig` (config.py:39-95), no CLI flag (cli.py:288-320), no UI control (index.html:178-186, app.js:180-181), and no way to inject it through the web API, since `/api/config` (server.py:996-1001) only accepts keys already present in the fixed `Session.settings` literal (server.py:103-113). Because guards.py:44/110/138 and strategies.py:39/49 deliberately forbid skip/xfail, there is also no quarantine path to clear the reds instead. The consequence is narrower but sharper than "mutation is unreachable": mutation still runs fine on a project whose own suite is green (`run --mutation` alone, as in the appdirs and cachetools runs), but a mutation score can never be obtained *in the same run that generated tests* once the repair ladder leaves even one failure — so `--all-phases` silently drops stage 8, and AUTEF cannot produce a before/after mutation delta attributable to its own generated tests. Note the gate's reasoning is sound (enhance.py:470-475): a 

### [major] Stage 8 has no time budget. `runner.py:104-107` gives each mutant's fail-fast verdict run a ceiling of `single_test_timeout_s * 8` = 960 s — larger than the 900
*blocks end-to-end: True · confidence 0.9*

**Evidence.** `runner.py:104-107`: `run_fail_fast` uses `timeout=self.config.single_test_timeout_s * 8`. `config.py:80-81`: `suite_timeout_s: int = 900`, `single_test_timeout_s: int = 120` — so each mutant gets 960 s, more than the whole suite is allowed. `enhance.py:415-442` loops over up to `max_mutants` sites with no aggregate budget and no per-site override; `web/static/index.html:180` lets `max_mutants` reach 100, i.e. a worst case of ~26 hours inside one stage.

**Why it matters.** This is exactly E3: the 20-mutant cachetools run took 1156 s versus 163 s for 10, consistent with one mutant (the `cond.wait_for(lambda: k not in pending)` deadlock) burning its entire 960 s slot. Stage 8 becomes unbounded and unpredictable, and there is no cancel (see the stage-cancellation finding), so the session is wedged for the duration.

**Fix sketch.** Add an explicit `mutant_timeout_s` (default well under `suite_timeout_s`, e.g. 60-120 s) used by `run_fail_fast`, plus a total wall-clock budget for `MutationPhase.score` that stops sampling and reports how many sites were skipped.

**Verifier's correction.** Stage 8 has no time budget. `runner.py:104-107` gives each mutant's fail-fast verdict run a ceiling of `single_test_timeout_s * 8` = 960 s — larger than the 900 s ceiling on a full-suite pytest invocation (`runner.py:74,78,82` with `config.py:80-81`), inverting the intent that the `-x` run be the cheap one. `pytest_arguments` (runner.py:148-169) adds no per-test timeout, so a single hanging test (e.g. a `cond.wait_for` deadlock introduced by a mutant) consumes the whole 960 s as a subprocess wall (runner.py:186-193). `enhance.py:415-442` loops over up to `max_mutants` sites with no aggregate deadline, no elapsed-time check and no per-site override, and `web/static/index.html:180` exposes `max_mutants` up to 100 — a worst case of ~26.7 h for the scoring pass alone, plus the `require_green` baseline suite and up to 5 survivors x 2 `run_file` calls at 480 s each (enhance.py:539,559). With no cancel path in `web/server.py`, the session is unusable for that duration. The E3 timings (163 s for 10 mutants vs 1156 s for 20) are consistent with one mutant burning its slot but are not per-mutant instrumented, so they corroborate rather than prove the mechanism; the code alone establishes the

### [major] Mutants the suite could not judge are counted in the mutation-score denominator as misses, and the report's own prose says they are excluded. enhance.py:419-421
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.9*

**Evidence.** `enhance.py:429-433` sets `mutant.error = "the suite did not run under this mutant"` (the timeout/crash case) and leaves `mutant.killed` False. `models.py:451-467`: `total = len(self.mutants)`, `killed = sum(1 for m in self.mutants if m.killed)`, `score = killed / total`, `survivors() = [m for m in self.mutants if not m.killed]` — none of these filter `m.error`. The serialiser comment at `server.py:612-614` asserts the opposite: "not a kill, and not a gap in the tests either, so it is excluded from the score". It is not excluded.
---
In `MutationPhase.score`, an apply failure sets `mutant.error = str(exc)` and appends the mutant (src/autef2/enhance.py:419-421), and a suite that will not run under the mutant sets `mutant.error = "the suite did not run under this mutant"` (enhance.py:433) while leaving `killed` False (models.py:431). `MutationSnapshot.survived = total - killed` and `score = killed/total` (src/autef2/models.py:458-465) count both in the denominator as misses. The kill agent filters them (`if not m.error`, enhance.py:488) but the reported score does not.

**Why it matters.** Every timed-out or unapplicable mutant silently deflates the reported mutation score and pads the survivor list, so the headline number in the report is not a mutation score at all. With a 960 s per-mutant timeout on a project with any blocking mutant (E3), this is not hypothetical. It also makes the before/after delta meaningless, since an errored mutant can never be "newly killed".

**Fix sketch.** Compute `total`/`survivors()` over `[m for m in mutants if not m.error]` and report the errored count separately as "not judged", matching the claim already made in `server.py:612-614`.

**Verifier's correction.** Mutants the suite could not judge are counted in the mutation-score denominator as misses, and the report's own prose says they are excluded. enhance.py:419-421 (apply failure) and enhance.py:430-433 (suite did not run, e.g. the 960 s run_fail_fast timeout at runner.py:106 + config.py:81) both set mutant.error and append the mutant with killed=False. models.py:450-465 computes total/killed/survived/score over all mutants with no m.error filter, and server.py:762-766 -> report.py:482-484 / app.js:569 publish those unfiltered values as the headline "Mutation score" and "Killed N / total". So 8 killed, 1 real survivor, 1 unscorable mutant reports 80% rather than the true 89%. The per-mutant table does filter (report.py:189-190, app.js:658-659) and labels such mutants "not scored", and report.py:225-228 tells the reader they "are excluded from the score" - a statement the headline on the same page contradicts. The unfiltered MutationSnapshot.survivors() also leaks unscorable mutants into the CLI's "Mutants still surviving" list (cli.py:150), though not into the HTML report or web UI. The before/after delta is not invalidated, only compressed, since both sides share the inflated denomin

### [major] Stage 8's green gate is unconditional and has no override, so any residual failing test — not just `production_bug`/`environment_dependency` skips — makes mutat
*blocks end-to-end: True · confidence 0.9*

**Evidence.** `orchestrator.py:159-165`: when `diagnosis.root_cause in NON_REPAIRABLE` (`models.py:40-42` = PRODUCTION_BUG, ENVIRONMENT_DEPENDENCY) the record gets a `skipped_reason` and returns — the test is left failing, untouched. `enhance.py:470-476` then counts it: `elif baseline.failures or baseline.collection_errors: outcome.skipped_reason = ...`. Nothing distinguishes "we chose not to repair this" from "we tried and failed".

**Why it matters.** E1's appdirs run had 2 tests diagnosed `production_bug`. Those two alone are enough to gate mutation off forever, on every subsequent re-run, no matter how many times the user retries — the diagnosis is deterministic-ish and the skip is terminal. The refusal to edit the test is correct; making it an unrecoverable pipeline stop is not.

**Fix sketch.** Track deliberately-skipped tests separately from unfixed ones and let the green gate treat a deliberate skip as accounted-for (ideally by xfail-marking it with the diagnosis as the reason), while still reporting it prominently as a suspected production defect.

**Verifier's correction.** Stage 8's green gate is unconditional and has no override, so any residual failing test — not just `production_bug`/`environment_dependency` skips — makes mutation testing unreachable. `enhance.py:463-479` re-runs the whole suite and returns early on `baseline.failures or baseline.collection_errors`; `require_green` defaults True (`enhance.py:458`) and no caller, CLI flag (`cli.py:305-321`), or config field ever sets it False. Deliberate skips (`orchestrator.py:159-165` for NON_REPAIRABLE per `models.py:40-42`, and `orchestrator.py:204-209` for a claimed source defect) and ladder-exhausted failures (`orchestrator.py:171-225`) all leave the test red on disk — nothing quarantines or removes it (`enhance.py:166-191` keeps failing generated files deliberately; `_remove_generated` at `enhance.py:607-620` is only used by the coverage and mutation-killer validators). The records themselves do distinguish "chose not to repair" from "tried and failed" via `skipped_reason`; the gate simply never reads them, and mutation scoring has no baseline-failure subtraction (`enhance.py:426-428` marks a mutant killed on any failure under `run_fail_fast`), so running against a red suite is not a safe fa

### [major] The mutation phase's green gate and its per-mutant scoring use two different, deliberately incompatible pytest invocation strategies, so the gate can certify a 
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.85*

**Evidence.** The gate at `enhance.py:464` calls `run_suite()`, which for multiple roots runs each root in a *separate* pytest invocation and merges (`runner.py:76-89`, `_merge_into` at `runner.py:344-372`, where `merged.ran = True` if *any* root ran and a dead root contributes no failures). Scoring at `enhance.py:425` calls `run_fail_fast()`, which puts every root into *one* invocation (`runner.py:104-107`, and the docstring at `runner.py:100-102` says so explicitly).
---
The gate at src/autef2/enhance.py:463-476 calls `self.runner.run_suite()`, which for multiple roots runs each root in a SEPARATE invocation and tolerates one that cannot start (src/autef2/runner.py:76-89 plus `_merge_into` at runner.py:344-364: `merged.ran = True` if ANY root ran). The scoring loop at enhance.py:425 calls `run_fail_fast()`, which deliberately puts every root into ONE invocation (runner.py:95-107, docstring lines 100-102: "Every root goes into one invocation here, unlike run_suite").

**Why it matters.** For a project with a test root it cannot import — precisely the `examples/*/tests` case `runner.py:66-71` was written to tolerate — the gate sees green (the dead root produced no failures and another root ran) but every `run_fail_fast` aborts at that root's conftest, so `result.ran` is False for all 20 mutants. Each is stamped `error` and, per the finding above, scored as a survivor. The run reports `measured=True` and a 0% mutation score that measured nothing.

**Fix sketch.** Have `run_fail_fast` iterate roots the same way `run_suite` does (stopping at the first root that produces a failure), or restrict mutation scoring to the roots that were green in the gating run.

**Verifier's correction.** The mutation phase's green gate and its per-mutant scoring use two different, deliberately incompatible pytest invocation strategies, so the gate can certify a suite green that the scoring loop cannot run at all — and the resulting mutation score is fabricated in either direction.

The gate (`src/autef2/enhance.py:463-476`) calls `run_suite()`, which for multiple test roots runs each root in a separate invocation and merges tolerantly (`runner.py:73-89`; `_merge_into` at `runner.py:344-372` sets `merged.ran = True` if any root ran, and ignores the failing root's returncode). The scoring loop (`enhance.py:425`) calls `run_fail_fast()`, which deliberately puts every root into ONE invocation (`runner.py:95-107`, docstring 100-102). No un-mutated baseline `run_fail_fast` is ever taken, so the phase never learns that its scoring command is broken independently of the mutants.

Two reproduced consequences (both verified live against the repo's own Flask fixture pattern from `tests_autef2/test_portability_and_ui.py:268-291`):

1. Unimportable conftest in one root (e.g. `examples/*/tests`): run_suite = ran/2 passed/0 failures (gate green, rc 4 ignored); run_fail_fast = ran=False, rc 4, not

### [major] Diagnosis is structurally blind to whether the failing test is AUTEF's own unreviewed stage-4 output — and the signal that would tell it exists and is deliberat
*blocks end-to-end: True · confidence 0.85*

**Evidence.** `grep -n "AUTEF_GENERATED|generated|authored" src/autef2/agents/failure_analysis.py src/autef2/context.py src/autef2/agents/repair_strategy.py` returns nothing. `orchestrator.py:260-266` builds the diagnostic context from `ContextBuilder.build_diagnostic(failure, span)` with no provenance flag, and `RepairOrchestrator` is handed the post-generation layout (`pipeline.py:91-93`) with no record of which files stage 4 wrote. Meanwhile the generated file's own header says it is "Reviewed by no one: treat as a starting point, not as a specification" (`generation.py:327-329`).

**Why it matters.** A test the model invented from the source, then failed to satisfy, can be diagnosed `production_bug` — the framework concluding the *project* is wrong on the strength of its own unreviewed guess (E1: 2 of 4 skips were production_bug on appdirs). That skip is terminal (previous finding) and gates mutation off. The same blindness drives E5's python-tabulate case, where the model cannot predict exact formatting and its own output is then treated as an oracle.

**Fix sketch.** Pass an `autef_authored: bool` through `TestFailure`/context (derivable from `generation.authored_here`) and instruct the Failure Analysis Agent that for an AUTEF-authored test, `production_bug` requires far stronger evidence and the default is that the generated expectation is wrong.

**Verifier's correction.** Diagnosis is structurally blind to whether the failing test is AUTEF's own unreviewed stage-4 output — and the signal that would tell it exists and is deliberately excluded. `GENERATED_MARKER`/`authored_here` (src/autef2/agents/generation.py:39,42-48) can answer the question with a file read and no model call, but are used only for path reservation (generation.py:70,77) and deleting statically-invalid files (generation.py:233, enhance.py:607-621). The "Reviewed by no one" banner (generation.py:325-330) cannot reach the diagnosis agent: `build_diagnostic` (context.py:78-92) omits `_test_file_block` (context.py:128-132, called only from the repair prompt at context.py:52-55), and `FunctionSpan.source` is the function extent only (patcher.py:34-47), so a module docstring is structurally excluded. The filename does not leak it either — `reserve_path` adds `_autef` only on collision (generation.py:73-79), and stage 4 targets untested modules, so the normal output is a plain `test_<module>.py`. Meanwhile `validate_generated` is static-only and explicitly routes failing generated tests to the repair loop (generation.py:254-259), and pipeline.py:91-93 hands `RepairOrchestrator` only the po

### [major] Stage 7's repair pass re-runs the full repair ladder on tests stage 6 already exhausted, and (on the web path) erases their stage-6 attempt history. Stage 7 is 
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.85*

**Evidence.** `server.py:419-424`: after writing coverage tests, stage 7 re-collects and, `if st.get("failures")`, calls `_repair_pass(session, config, merge=True)`. `_repair_pass` (`server.py:375-397`) iterates *every* currently failing test — not just the new coverage ones — and for each does `diagnosed = orchestrator.diagnose(failure)` (a fresh `RepairRecord`, `orchestrator.py:133-136`) then `records[failure.nodeid] = orchestrator.repair(...)`, replacing the stage-6 entry wholesale.

### [minor] On the two defensive rejection paths in `_kills` — `site is None` (enhance.py:549-551) and `MutationError` on reapply (enhance.py:553-557) — the record is left 
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.9*

**Evidence.** `enhance.py:548-558`: both the `site is None` branch and the `MutationError` branch set `record.error` and `return False` *without* calling `_remove_generated(record)` — so the file stays on disk and `record.accepted` remains True (contrast `enhance.py:545` and `571`, which do call it). Consequences: `pipeline.py:239-244` counts it via `kept = [g for g in report.mutation_generated if g.accepted]` and prints it as "verified killer test(s)"; `server.py:769` reports `"written": len(mut.accepted)`, rendered as "Killer tests kept" in `app.js:572` and `report.py:487`; and `server.py:621-623` suppresses the error (`record.error if ... and not record.accepted else None`), so the UI shows no reason at all.

**Why it matters.** It inverts the exact honesty guarantee `_kills`' docstring makes (`enhance.py:529-534`). A test that was never shown to kill anything is presented to the reader — and to the thesis — as a verified killer, and the failure reason is hidden in precisely the UI that exists to answer "one survived, what happened".

**Fix sketch.** Call `_remove_generated(record)` (or at minimum set `record.accepted = False`) on both un-verifiable branches, so an unverified killer test is reported as rejected with its reason.

**Verifier's correction.** On the two defensive rejection paths in `_kills` — `site is None` (enhance.py:549-551) and `MutationError` on reapply (enhance.py:553-557) — the record is left with `accepted=True` because neither calls `_remove_generated` (contrast enhance.py:545 and 571, and note that enhance.py:621 is the only place `accepted` is cleared). A killer test whose kill could never be verified is therefore counted in the "verified killer test(s)" line (pipeline.py:239-244) and in the "Killer tests kept" metric (server.py:768 -> app.js:572, report.py:487), and its `record.error` is suppressed in the mutant table because server.py:621-623 only surfaces the error when `not record.accepted` — so the row that exists to answer "one survived, what happened" says `attempted: true` with no reason. The generated file also stays on disk labelled "kept" in the file viewer, though contradictorily with its error text shown (server.py:569, app.js:379). Scope limits: the reported mutation score is NOT inflated (no `_mark_killed`, since `_kills` returns False), and the retained file does not redden the suite (it already passed on unmutated source at enhance.py:536-546). Both branches are defensive and unreached by any

*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.85*

Nothing in the report records which stages actually ran, so the artefact is indistinguishable from a full run.

### [minor] In the web UI only, `_worker` clears the whole queue when any stage other than 5 or 6 errors (src\autef2\web\server.py:511-515, 521-527), so a run-all that hits
*blocks end-to-end: True · confidence 0.85*

**Evidence.** `server.py:487-506` `_run_stage` catches `StageError` and `Exception` into `session.error`. `server.py:508-527` `_worker`: `if session.error or not session.queue: session.queue = []; ... return`. Only stages 5 and 6 are skippable (`server.py:521-522`); every other blocked or failing stage kills the queue. `_collect_suite` raises `StageError` on a non-running or timed-out suite (`server.py:218-228`), and it is called from stage 4 (`server.py:288`) and stage 7 (`server.py:419`, `423`) as well as stage 3.

**Why it matters.** A suite that times out or fails to collect *after* generation wrote files aborts at stage 4 — the generated files stay on disk (`GenerationPhase._validate` at `enhance.py:166-201` only reverts on a per-file failure, never on suite-wide breakage), stages 7, 8 and 9 never run, and the user is left with a modified project and no report. The all-or-nothing queue means one recoverable stage failure costs the entire run.

**Fix sketch.** Mark stages 4, 7 and 8 as non-fatal in a chained run — record the stage error in `session.state` and continue to stage 9 — so a report is always produced and names which stages failed and why.

**Verifier's correction.** In the web UI only, `_worker` clears the whole queue when any stage other than 5 or 6 errors (src\autef2\web\server.py:511-515, 521-527), so a run-all that hits a `StageError` from `_collect_suite` at stage 4 (server.py:287) or stage 7 (server.py:420, 424) stops the chain rather than continuing to the report. The report itself is still obtainable - `report.build()` works off the session snapshot and the download routes need only stage 1 (web\report.py:35-37; server.py:941-946) - and the user can resume by clicking stage 9 alone, since `_start` clears `session.error` (server.py:536) and `_stage_report` never raises (server.py:445-450). The actual user-visible defect is that app.js renders the download buttons only when `state.after` exists (static\app.js:578, 596-600), so after an aborted chain there is no visible route to the partial report, and the generated tests left in the workspace copy have no batch rollback (`revert` is per-file only, enhance.py:181, 191). The original project on disk is not modified - ingest works on a copy (ingest.py:99-109). The CLI path is unaffected: `run_project` always returns a RunReport (pipeline.py:99-137, orchestrator.py:88-94).

### [minor] The timeout StageError at server.py:219-224 advises "Raise the timeout, or choose a smaller project", but no shipped interface lets a user raise it: _config (se
*blocks end-to-end: True · confidence 0.85*

**Evidence.** `server.py:219-224` raises `StageError("The suite was still running after {suite_timeout_s}s ... Raise the timeout, or choose a smaller project.")`. But `_config` (`server.py:178-190`) builds the config from only model, reasoning_effort, max_attempts, use_venv and use_signature_cache; `AutefConfig.from_env` (`config.py:130-154`) recognises no timeout variable at all; and `index.html:117-186` has no timeout control. `suite_timeout_s` (`config.py:80`) can only be changed by editing source.

**Why it matters.** The one recovery action the pipeline suggests when a large project's suite times out is impossible to perform. Since a timed-out `_collect_suite` raises `StageError`, this also aborts the whole run-all chain (see that finding), so the project is simply out of scope with no recourse.

**Fix sketch.** Read `AUTEF_SUITE_TIMEOUT_S` / `AUTEF_SINGLE_TEST_TIMEOUT_S` in `AutefConfig.from_env`, expose a timeout control in the settings panel, and thread it through `_config`.

**Verifier's correction.** The timeout StageError at server.py:219-224 advises "Raise the timeout, or choose a smaller project", but no shipped interface lets a user raise it: _config (server.py:179-192) threads only five settings through; AutefConfig.from_env (config.py:130-154) recognises no timeout environment variable; the web settings panel (index.html:110-186) has no timeout control; and cli.py:265-371 defines no --suite-timeout flag. suite_timeout_s (config.py:80) is reachable only by editing source or by a Python caller passing it as a keyword override to from_env (config.py:131,153). So the first of the two suggested recovery actions is unactionable for every actual user of the tool; the second ("choose a smaller project") is performable, so the message is half-dead rather than wholly useless. This is a misleading-message defect only — it does not itself block a run; the abort comes from the timeout and the StageError (see that separate finding), and rewording this sentence would not unblock anything.

*blocks end-to-end: True · confidence 0.95*

The removal helpers exist but run only at validation or mutation-rejection time: `_remove_generated` at enhance.py:607-621 is called from enhance.py:328/337/545/571, and `TestGenerationAgent.revert` (agents/generation.py:227-238) from enhance.py:181/191. `GENERATED_MARKER` is never read at the end of a run. ### [major] For layout_style "package", AUTEF derives the wrong dotted import name and then enforces it. `_detect_source_roots` (src/autef2/ingest.py:507-517) returns the p
*blocks end-to-end: True · confidence 0.9*

### [minor] Coverage measures all test roots in one pytest invocation (src/autef2/coverage_tool.py:105-114) while run_suite deliberately runs them one at a time (src/autef2
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.7*

The claim at src/autef2/runner.py:149-155 that the flags are "shared with the coverage run so the two cannot drift" covers the flags but not the invocation strategy.

**Fix sketch.** Run coverage per root with --data-file fragments and combine, mirroring run_suite.

**Verifier's correction.** Coverage measures all test roots in one pytest invocation (src/autef2/coverage_tool.py:105-114) while run_suite deliberately runs them one at a time (src/autef2/runner.py:63-89) precisely because one unimportable conftest aborts a combined invocation. On a multi-root project with one unrunnable root, the suite reports a correct partial result but the coverage child dies at initial-conftest load; coverage still writes its data file on that exit, so coverage_tool.py:122-154 sets measured=True and enhance.py:274-278's guard passes, producing a silent near-0% coverage_before/after over source roots that were never imported (and making gaps() nominate every file) rather than a reported error. The "cannot drift" comment at runner.py:149-155 unifies flags only, not the invocation strategy; contrast run_fail_fast (runner.py:95-107), which documents its single-invocation choice as intentional.

### [major] Per-mutant budget (960 s) exceeds the whole-suite timeout (900 s), and the mutation phase has no aggregate cap. src/autef2/enhance.py:425 calls runner.run_fail_
*blocks end-to-end: True · confidence 0.95*

The scoring loop (enhance.py:415-442) has no wall-clock budget, and neither timeout is exposed via CLI or AutefConfig.from_env (config.py:131-152), so it cannot be lowered without editing source. With defaults the phase can legitimately take ~6.4 h: 20 x 960 s scoring, plus 900 s per test root for the green gate (run_suite times out per root, runner.py:82), plus 2 x 480 s per survivor for kill verification (enhance.py:536/560 via runner.py:112, max_survivors=5). It does terminate — a timed-out mutant is caught at runner.py:205-227 and recorded at enhance.py:430-433 — so stage 9 is still reached; the failure mode is a multi-hour stall, not a hang. ### [major] AUTEF v2's mutation engine implements four operator families only. `src/autef2/mutation.py:127-143` dispatches on `ast.Compare`, `ast.BinOp`, `ast.BoolOp` and `
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.95*

**Evidence.** src/autef2/mutation.py:127-145 dispatches on exactly four node types: `ast.Compare`, `ast.BinOp`, `ast.BoolOp`, `ast.Constant`. Tables at mutation.py:40-68 cover comparisons, `+ - * / // % **`, and `and/or`. Verified empirically this session on a synthetic module: `total += 1` (ast.AugAssign) produced only the literal mutation and no `+= -> -=`; `n = -x` (ast.UnaryOp/USub) produced nothing; `flag = not x` (ast.Not) produced nothing; `break`/`continue` produced nothing; `return x & y` (ast.BitAnd) produced nothing. Strings and None are excluded by design at mutation.py:238-239.

**Why it matters.** The absent operators are the ones mutation-testing literature treats as core: unary-negation of conditions, statement deletion, return-value replacement, break<->continue, boundary/off-by-one on slices. Because `ast.AugAssign` is not an `ast.BinOp`, accumulator code (`total += x`) is completely unmutated - a large class of real logic. The mutation score therefore measures a narrow operator set, which is a weak base for the thesis' claim about suite sensitivity and for any v1-vs-v2 comparison.

**Fix sketch.** Add AugAssign op swaps (reusing _ARITHMETIC_SWAPS), bitwise swaps, `not`-insertion on if/while tests, `return <expr>` -> `return None`, break<->continue, and slice/index +-1; name each operator class in the report so the score can be broken down by class.

**Verifier's correction.** AUTEF v2's mutation engine implements four operator families only. `src/autef2/mutation.py:127-143` dispatches on `ast.Compare`, `ast.BinOp`, `ast.BoolOp` and `ast.Constant`; the swap tables at mutation.py:40-68 admit only the ten relational/identity/membership flips, `+ - * / // % **`, and `and`/`or`. Absent are unary-operator mutation (`not x`, `-x`, `~x`), statement deletion, return-value replacement, and break<->continue — none of those node types appear anywhere in src/ . Bitwise operators reach `_operator_site` as `ast.BinOp` but are dropped at mutation.py:184-186 because they are not in `_ARITHMETIC_SWAPS`. Most concretely, `ast.AugAssign` is not an `ast.BinOp`, so `total += x` produces no mutation site at all (verified: a synthetic module with `+=`, `break`, `continue`, `-x`, `not x`, `~x`, and `& | ^ >> <<` yielded only comparison flips and integer-literal increments). Two corrections to the original wording: slice off-by-one IS produced when the index is a literal, via the integer `value + 1` rule at mutation.py:234-235 (`xs[1:]` -> `xs[2:]`); and the limitation is disclosed rather than overclaimed — AUTEF_V2_EXPLAINED.md:271-274 and the mutation.py:14-19 docstring both d

### [major] Mutation testing re-runs the entire test suite for every mutant and samples mutation sites blindly, with no per-test coverage to restrict either. `MutationPhase
*blocks end-to-end: True · confidence 0.93*

**Evidence.** src/autef2/enhance.py:425 calls `self.runner.run_fail_fast()` with no targets; src/autef2/runner.py:104 `args = list(targets) if targets else self._default_targets()` and runner.py:116-118 `_default_targets` returns every test root. Nothing passes coverage data into the mutation phase: src/autef2/mutation.py:95-110 `sites()` takes only `limit` and `seed`, and MutationPhase.__init__ (enhance.py:386-399) receives no CoverageSnapshot even though CoveragePhase ran earlier in the same pipeline (src/autef2/pipeline.py:99-113).

**Why it matters.** Cost is O(mutants x full suite). Every mainstream mutation tool (mutmut, cosmic-ray, pytest-mutagen) restricts each mutant to the tests that execute the mutated line; AUTEF does not, which is the direct cause of E3's 163 s -> 1156 s blow-up and of the 20-mutant cap that the HOD criticised as too small. The `-x` fail-fast only helps mutants that are killed early; every surviving mutant pays for the entire suite.

**Fix sketch.** Run coverage once with per-test line attribution (coverage.py contexts / `--cov-context=test`), then for each mutant pass only the nodeids that touch `site.file:site.lineno` into run_fail_fast; a mutant on a line no test reaches is a survivor by construction and needs no run at all.

**Verifier's correction.** Mutation testing re-runs the entire test suite for every mutant and samples mutation sites blindly, with no per-test coverage to restrict either. `MutationPhase.score` calls `self.runner.run_fail_fast()` with no targets once per mutant (src/autef2/enhance.py:425 in the loop at 415-442); with `targets=None` the runner falls back to `_default_targets()`, every test root (src/autef2/runner.py:104, 116-118). `Mutator.sites()` selects sites by count and seed alone (src/autef2/mutation.py:95-109), and `MutationPhase.__init__` receives no coverage information (src/autef2/enhance.py:386-399; constructed at src/autef2/pipeline.py:117 despite CoveragePhase having run at pipeline.py:99-103). The `-x` flag (runner.py:105) short-circuits only mutants that are killed early; survivors — 18/20 on appdirs — each pay a full suite run, giving an O(mutants x suite) floor of ~16 s/mutant on cachetools and forcing the 20-mutant cap the HOD criticised as too small. ### [major] Mutation score is reported only as a point estimate with no sampling uncertainty. `models.py:461-463` computes `killed / total` and every surface renders it as 
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.93*

**Evidence.** src/autef2/models.py:462-464 `score = self.killed / self.total`; src/autef2/web/report.py:482-484 `_metric("Mutation score", f"{mut.get('score_after')}%", f"was {mut.get('score_before')}%")` and server.py:762-763 `round((before.score if before else 0) * 100)`; src/autef2/pipeline.py:240-244 prints the same ratio. Nothing computes an interval - a grep for `confidence`/`interval`/`wilson` across src/autef2/ hits only the diagnosis agent's per-failure confidence (agents/failure_analysis.py).

**Why it matters.** E3's headline '85% -> 90%' is 17/20 -> 18/20: one mutant. A Wilson 95% interval on 18/20 is roughly 70-97%, which would make it obvious that a 5-point 'improvement' from n=20 is inside the noise. The thesis currently quotes point estimates from a sample the HOD already called too small, with no way for a reader to see the width.

**Fix sketch.** Compute a Wilson score interval from killed/total in MutationSnapshot and render 'x% (95% CI a-b%, n=N)' in the report, CLI summary and UI; flag score_gain as not significant when the intervals overlap.

**Verifier's correction.** Mutation score is reported only as a point estimate with no sampling uncertainty. `models.py:461-463` computes `killed / total` and every surface renders it as a percentage plus a percentage-point delta — `pipeline.py:240-243`, `web/server.py:762-763`, `web/report.py:482-483`, `web/static/app.js:568`. The raw killed/total counts *are* shown alongside (`report.py:485`, `app.js:569`, `pipeline.py:242`), so n is visible, but nothing computes a confidence interval or standard error, and the "+N pts" delta is presented as an improvement with no indication of its width. This matters more than a presentation nit because the mutant set is a random sample of mutation sites, not a census (`mutation.py:95-109`, default 20 via `cli.py:318`), so the reported figure is an estimate of the project-wide score. The codebase already does exact small-sample inference for the repair comparison (`eval/compare.py:113-126`, exact binomial McNemar), so mutation and coverage are the inconsistent cases; `eval/metrics.py` carries no mutation field at all.

### [major] Mutant identity omits the column offset, so two mutation sites on the same line with the same operator collapse to one identity — and the kill-verification step
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.92*

**Evidence.** src/autef2/models.py:426-428: `Mutant` carries only `file`, `lineno`, `operator` - no column. `MutationSite.key()` (src/autef2/mutation.py:83-84) does include `col_offset`, so two distinct sites collapse to one Mutant identity. Matching is done on that identity in src/autef2/enhance.py:581-589 `_site_for` (returns the FIRST site matching file/lineno/operator), enhance.py:636-647 `_mark_killed` (marks the FIRST match and `return`s), and src/autef2/web/server.py:599-602 `attempt_for = {(m.file, m.lineno, m.operator): record ...}`. Verified this session: for the line `if x == 1 or y == 2:` the mutator emitted two sites at cols 9 and 19, both with operator `comparison == -> !=`; likewise two `comparison > -> <=` and two `literal 0 -> 1` on `if x > 0 and y > 0:`.

**Why it matters.** `_kills` re-applies `_site_for(...)` to verify the killer test, so when two sites share an identity the test written for the second occurrence is verified against the FIRST one. A test can be accepted as 'verified' having killed a mutant it was not written for, and `_mark_killed` then flips the wrong mutant's flag. The verification that is v2's headline contribution over v1 quietly does not hold for any line with two same-operator sites - and such lines are common (`a == b or c == d`, `0 <= x < n`).

**Fix sketch.** Add `col_offset` to Mutant, populate it in `Mutator.describe`, and key `_site_for`, `_mark_killed` and the server's `attempt_for` on it; or carry the MutationSite object itself through the phase instead of re-deriving it.

**Verifier's correction.** Mutant identity omits the column offset, so two mutation sites on the same line with the same operator collapse to one identity — and the kill-verification step resolves the wrong one. `Mutant` (src/autef2/models.py:422-435) has file/lineno/operator but no column, while `MutationSite.key()` (src/autef2/mutation.py:83-84) includes `col_offset`; `Mutator.describe()` (mutation.py:344-350) drops it. `_site_for` (src/autef2/enhance.py:581-589) and `_mark_killed` (enhance.py:636-647) both match on file/lineno/operator and take the FIRST hit, and `attempt_for` in src/autef2/web/server.py:599-602 keys on the same triple (so one of two colliding attempt records is silently overwritten). Reproduced: `if x == 1 or y == 2:` emits two `comparison == -> !=` sites (cols 9, 19) with different mutated lines; `if x > 0 and y > 0:` emits two `comparison > -> <=` and two `literal 0 -> 1`; 168 of 2673 sites in AUTEF's own source (13%) belong to a colliding identity. Unlike the original claim, the fault does NOT fire for every such line: `_site_for` re-derives the same sampled site list (enhance.py:582 vs 408), so a twin that was not sampled cannot be confused. It fires only when both twins are in the s

### [major] Surviving mutants are never classified, so unkillable ones are counted as test gaps. `Mutant` (src/autef2/models.py:422-438) carries no equivalence or reachabil
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.9*

**Evidence.** No code path anywhere handles equivalence: `Mutant` (src/autef2/models.py:423-435) has fields `killed`, `killed_by`, `killed_after_generation`, `error` and nothing else; `MutationSnapshot.survivors()` (models.py:466-467) treats every non-killed mutant identically. The only mention is prose in src/autef2/web/report.py:220-223 ("unless the change cannot alter behaviour at all (an equivalent mutant), which no test can catch") with no mechanism behind it. A grep for `equivalent` across src/autef2/ returns only that string.

**Why it matters.** E2 is the direct consequence: 18 of 20 appdirs mutants survived and most were `system == "win32"`-style platform branches that cannot be killed on one OS. They are reported as test gaps, the agent spends model calls trying to kill them, and the mutation score reads as 10% when the achievable ceiling on that OS is far lower. Every conclusion drawn from the score inherits this bias.

**Fix sketch.** At minimum add an `equivalent`/`unkillable` flag with a reason, set it heuristically (comparisons against `sys.platform`/`os.name`/`__name__`, mutants on lines with zero coverage, mutants whose mutated AST is semantically identical) and exclude flagged mutants from the score with a visible count; allow the user to mark one from the UI.

**Verifier's correction.** Surviving mutants are never classified, so unkillable ones are counted as test gaps. `Mutant` (src/autef2/models.py:422-438) carries no equivalence or reachability field, `MutationSnapshot.survivors()` (models.py:466-467) returns every non-killed mutant undifferentiated, and `score` (models.py:463-464) is a flat `killed/total` with no adjusted denominator. Mutant generation applies no reachability or platform filter (mutation.py:117-173 flips every comparison it finds), `config.py` exposes no skip/exclusion list, and `build_mutation_prompt` (agents/mutation_kill.py:36-81) gives the model no way to report that a mutant cannot be killed, so enhance.py:488-509 spends a paid call on each one. Two distinct unkillable classes are affected and neither is handled: true equivalent mutants (no behavioural change, undecidable in general, so the realistic remedy is a manual label plus denominator adjustment) and environment-unreachable mutants such as appdirs' `system == "win32"` branches (E2: 18/20 survived, most of this kind), which do change behaviour but cannot be exercised on the host OS and could be flagged heuristically from platform guards or coverage. *blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.9*

**Evidence.** src/autef2/enhance.py:591-599 `_existing_tests_for` only matches `Path(candidate).name in (f"test_{stem}.py", f"{stem}_test.py")` and otherwise returns `""`. src/autef2/agents/mutation_kill.py:44-54 then emits `"\nThis module has no tests yet.\n"` for the empty case.

**Why it matters.** Real projects rarely name tests one-to-one with modules - cachetools' `_cached.py` has no `test__cached.py`, appdirs' tests live in one file. The model is told, falsely, that nothing tests the module, so it gets no sight of the suite's import style, fixtures or helper factories, and it re-derives an entry point from scratch. That is a direct contributor to the rejected killer tests in E2/E3, and it also defeats the stated purpose of the 'tests that did NOT catch this change' section (mutation_kill.py:47).

**Fix sketch.** Select existing tests by what actually imports the module (or by coverage attribution for the mutated line) rather than by filename, and fall back to the nearest test file in the same test root before claiming the module is untested.

**Verifier's correction.** Both LLM enhancement stages tell the model "This module has no tests yet" whenever the project's tests are not named one-to-one with the module file. `_existing_tests_for` is duplicated verbatim at src/autef2/enhance.py:339-348 (coverage, stage 7) and src/autef2/enhance.py:591-599 (mutation, stage 8); each scans `self.layout.test_files` and accepts a candidate only if its basename is exactly `test_<stem>.py` or `<stem>_test.py`, otherwise returns `""`. Those are the only two call sites (enhance.py:297, enhance.py:502) and there is no fallback — no conftest, no sibling test file, no import-graph or content-based match anywhere in src/autef2/agents/. The empty string is then rendered as the literal sentence "\nThis module has no tests yet.\n" in src/autef2/agents/mutation_kill.py:44-54 and as the same sentence in src/autef2/agents/coverage_improve.py:63-73. The statement is false by construction in stage 8: a mutant only exists for a module the just-executed suite ran against. It misfires on exactly the benchmark projects in benchmarks/manifest.json — appdirs (module `appdirs.py`, tests under `test/test_api.py`; the manifest itself notes "3 tests, main module untested") and cachetool

### [major] The killer-test prompt gives the model too little context to locate the mutated code, and silently truncates it away on larger modules. src/autef2/agents/mutati
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.88*

**Evidence.** src/autef2/agents/mutation_kill.py:56-68 is the whole context: import name, `Path(mutant.file).name`, line number, operator string, `before:`/`after:` single stripped lines (produced by mutation.py:348-349 `original=line.strip()`), then `Full source of the module:\n```python\n{source[:10000]}\n```` . The 10000 is hardcoded and ignores `AutefConfig.max_source_chars: int = 12_000` (src/autef2/config.py:88), which is what every other agent honours. No enclosing function or class name is passed, no surrounding lines, no conftest.py, no fixture list, no example test from the project.

**Why it matters.** For a module over ~10 KB the mutated line is simply absent from the prompt, so the model is asked to kill a change it cannot see in context. Even below the cut, `cond.wait_for(lambda: k not in pending)` on its own does not say which public function reaches it, so the model has to guess an entry point - which is precisely how you get a test that fails on the unmutated source (E2, E3).

**Fix sketch.** Use config.max_source_chars, and window the source around `mutant.lineno` rather than taking a prefix; include the enclosing def/class chain, the module's conftest fixtures, and one existing test from the project as a style/import example.

**Verifier's correction.** The killer-test prompt gives the model too little context to locate the mutated code, and silently truncates it away on larger modules. src/autef2/agents/mutation_kill.py:56-82 passes only the import name, file basename, line number, operator, and two stripped single lines (from src/autef2/mutation.py:347-348), followed by a raw `source[:10000]` character slice (mutation_kill.py:67). The model is never told which function or class encloses the mutated line — `Mutant` (src/autef2/models.py:423-435) and `MutationSite` (src/autef2/mutation.py:72-81) do not record it — nor given surrounding lines, conftest, fixtures, or an example test. Three consequences, all measured: (1) On appdirs.py (24,526 chars, 603 lines) the 10,000-char cut keeps only 223 lines, and 16 of the 20 sites returned by `Mutator.sites(limit=20, seed=1337)` fall beyond it, so the mutated line is absent from the prompt for 80% of the mutants in the run reported in E2. (2) The slice cuts mid-line with no truncation marker, unlike the unit-aligned `# ...[truncated]` in src/autef2/chunker.py:71-81, so the model cannot tell the source was cut. ### [major] The mutation phase's green gate and its per-mutant scoring use two different, deliberately incompatible pytest invocation strategies, so the gate can certify a 
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.85*

**Evidence.** The gate at `enhance.py:464` calls `run_suite()`, which for multiple roots runs each root in a *separate* pytest invocation and merges (`runner.py:76-89`, `_merge_into` at `runner.py:344-372`, where `merged.ran = True` if *any* root ran and a dead root contributes no failures). Scoring at `enhance.py:425` calls `run_fail_fast()`, which puts every root into *one* invocation (`runner.py:104-107`, and the docstring at `runner.py:100-102` says so explicitly).
---
The gate at src/autef2/enhance.py:463-476 calls `self.runner.run_suite()`, which for multiple roots runs each root in a SEPARATE invocation and tolerates one that cannot start (src/autef2/runner.py:76-89 plus `_merge_into` at runner.py:344-364: `merged.ran = True` if ANY root ran). The scoring loop at enhance.py:425 calls `run_fail_fast()`, which deliberately puts every root into ONE invocation (runner.py:95-107, docstring lines 100-102: "Every root goes into one invocation here, unlike run_suite").

**Why it matters.** For a project with a test root it cannot import — precisely the `examples/*/tests` case `runner.py:66-71` was written to tolerate — the gate sees green (the dead root produced no failures and another root ran) but every `run_fail_fast` aborts at that root's conftest, so `result.ran` is False for all 20 mutants. Each is stamped `error` and, per the finding above, scored as a survivor. The run reports `measured=True` and a 0% mutation score that measured nothing.

**Fix sketch.** Have `run_fail_fast` iterate roots the same way `run_suite` does (stopping at the first root that produces a failure), or restrict mutation scoring to the roots that were green in the gating run.

**Verifier's correction.** The mutation phase's green gate and its per-mutant scoring use two different, deliberately incompatible pytest invocation strategies, so the gate can certify a suite green that the scoring loop cannot run at all — and the resulting mutation score is fabricated in either direction.

The gate (`src/autef2/enhance.py:463-476`) calls `run_suite()`, which for multiple test roots runs each root in a separate invocation and merges tolerantly (`runner.py:73-89`; `_merge_into` at `runner.py:344-372` sets `merged.ran = True` if any root ran, and ignores the failing root's returncode). The scoring loop (`enhance.py:425`) calls `run_fail_fast()`, which deliberately puts every root into ONE invocation (`runner.py:95-107`, docstring 100-102). No un-mutated baseline `run_fail_fast` is ever taken, so the phase never learns that its scoring command is broken independently of the mutants.

Two reproduced consequences (both verified live against the repo's own Flask fixture pattern from `tests_autef2/test_portability_and_ui.py:268-291`):

1. Unimportable conftest in one root (e.g. `examples/*/tests`): run_suite = ran/2 passed/0 failures (gate green, rc 4 ignored); run_fail_fast = ran=False, rc 4, not

*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.85*

Each flip is individually verified — `_kills` really applies the mutant and requires pass-clean/fail-mutated (enhance.py:542, 554, 561) — so the score is not fabricated per mutant, but it is assembled, never re-measured as a set. The verdict at enhance.py:426 is `mutant.killed = bool(result.failures or result.collection_errors)`.

The consequence is NOT a false kill in the usual case: for the canonical `sys.exit(main())` body, pytest aborts with INTERNALERROR rather than a collection error, the autef plugin records nothing, src/autef2/runner.py:228-232 sets `ran=False`, and src/autef2/enhance.py:430-434 explicitly declines to score it as a kill ("the suite did not run under this mutant"). A false kill occurs only in the narrower case where the guard body raises a non-SystemExit exception, which does yield `collection_errors=1` and is scored killed at enhance.py:426. ### [minor] `_site_for` redundantly recomputes the full mutation-site sample. src/autef2/enhance.py:581-589 calls `self.mutator.sites(limit=max_mutants, seed=seed)` to reco
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.85*

It also makes the phase's correctness depend on `sites()` being perfectly reproducible across calls (file contents unchanged, same seed) rather than on a stored object - if a previously accepted killer test or a restore failure changed a source file in between, the recomputed sample can differ and `_site_for` returns None ('the mutant could not be reapplied to verify the kill', enhance.py:550).

The re-derivation is needed only because `Mutant` (src/autef2/models.py:423-435) stores no `col_offset`; keeping the line-408 sites in a dict keyed by (file, lineno, operator) would remove it. This is pure redundancy, not a correctness hazard: generated killer tests are written to test paths excluded from `_source_files` by `TEST_FILE_RE` (agents/mutation_kill.py:170-179, chunker.py:245, ingest.py:46) so they cannot perturb the sample; `restore` raises rather than silently diverging (mutation.py:323-324, called in a `finally` at enhance.py:435); and any stale site fails safe via `_site_for` returning None (enhance.py:548-551) or `apply`'s original_text guard raising `MutationError` (mutation.py:299-305). ### [blocker] Tests left red by the repair ladder (attempts exhausted, no strategy left) or skipped before it (NON_REPAIRABLE diagnosis at orchestrator.py:159-165, source_def
*blocks end-to-end: True · confidence 0.97*

No code path anywhere marks, xfails, deletes or moves such a test: `grep -rn "unlink|xfail|quarantin" src/autef2/` returns only agents/generation.py:234 and enhance.py:618 (generation-time whole-file revert) and guards.py (detection only). enhance.py:165-172 states outright "A file is kept even when its tests fail: that is the repair loop's input." Session evidence E1 (3 NOT FIXED + 4 SKIPPED -> stage 8 gated off) and E5 (python-tabulate).

**Why it matters.** This is the user's core complaint mechanised. A single test that the ladder cannot repair — or that one model call labels production_bug — permanently prevents stages 8 and a green stage 9 for the whole project. The pipeline has a producer of red tests (stage 4 generation, kept even when failing) and no consumer of last resort. Portability claims are worthless if any generated test the repairer cannot fix bricks the rest of the run.

**Fix sketch.** After the repair loop, quarantine residual failures: rewrite each unrepaired/skipped test with `@pytest.mark.xfail(strict=False, reason=<diagnosis>)` (or move it to a `quarantine/` dir excluded from the mutation baseline), record the list in the report, and let MutationPhase's require_green ignore xfailed nodes.

**Verifier's correction.** Tests left red by the repair ladder (attempts exhausted, no strategy left) or skipped before it (NON_REPAIRABLE diagnosis at orchestrator.py:159-165, source_defect_claimed at orchestrator.py:204-209) are never quarantined: the file is restored to its red original (agents/autofix.py:167/177/183) and only a skipped_reason string is recorded. Stage 4 and stage 7 deliberately keep failing generated files (enhance.py:166-172, 174-201, 320-336), so the pipeline produces red tests with the repair ladder as its only consumer. Stage 8 then refuses to run against a red suite (enhance.py:463-481), and require_green has no CLI flag, no config key and no caller override (only occurrences are enhance.py:458 and 463; pipeline.py:117-122 and web/server.py:429-436 use the default). Net effect: one unrepairable generated test permanently gates off mutation testing for the whole project. Two precisions to the original wording: a last-resort deletion path does exist but only for stage 8's own killer tests (enhance.py:545, 571 call _remove_generated), never for stage 4/7 output or for a test the ladder failed on; and stage 9 still runs and emits a report, it just reports a red suite with mutation unmea

**Evidence.** failure_analysis.py:82-83, rule: `re.compile(r"(could not connect to server|OperationalError|Access denied for user|no such table|Redis|Kafka|docker)", re.I), RootCause.ENVIRONMENT_DEPENDENCY, 0.75`. failure_analysis.py:109 builds the haystack as `f"{failure.exception_message}\n{failure.longrepr}"`, and runner.py:320 fills longrepr from pytest's rendered longrepr, which includes the *source lines of the failing test*. Verified live: a plain AssertionError whose test is named `test_docker_compose_defaults` classifies as (ENVIRONMENT_DEPENDENCY, 0.75); so does `assert 'redis://x' == 'redis://y'`. These rules are scanned (failure_analysis.py:114-116) before the exception-type table, so they beat ASSERTION_MISMATCH. 0.75 < 0.8 so the heuristic-wins guard at failure_analysis.py:200 never rescues it, and environment_dependency is in NON_REPAIRABLE (models.py:40-42).

**Why it matters.** A test whose name or asserted string merely mentions a common infrastructure word is diagnosed as an environment problem, permanently skipped, and left red — which then gates mutation off (finding 1). The classifier is described at failure_analysis.py:7-13 as 'right on its own for the unambiguous cases'; these substrings are not unambiguous, and they route straight into the one irreversible bucket.

**Fix sketch.** Match environment signatures against `exception_message` only (not the rendered source), require an exception type consistent with I/O (ConnectionError/OSError/OperationalError), and never let a regex alone select a NON_REPAIRABLE cause.

**Verifier's correction.** The ENVIRONMENT_DEPENDENCY heuristic at failure_analysis.py:83 matches the bare, unanchored substrings "Redis", "Kafka" and "docker" (plus "OperationalError", "no such table") anywhere in exception_message + longrepr. The rules are scanned at failure_analysis.py:114-116 before the exception-type table (failure_analysis.py:121-123), so they beat ASSERTION_MISMATCH. Two exploit paths survive that check: (1) the model keeps the parametrize decorator but shrinks its case list to just the failing case, so the target nodeid still passes while the other cases are silently deleted — compare sees an unchanged assertion profile and detect_regressions cannot see vanished cases; (2) under scope="file" strategies (strategies.py:112,126,136,221,241,322), replace_file (patcher.py:162-172) validates only that the text parses and never counts test functions, so a whole-file rewrite that deletes sibling test functions is accepted as a clean verified fix. ### [major] The NO_TEST_FIX_NEEDED opt-out is an unanchored substring test (`autef2/agents/autofix.py:135`, `if NO_FIX_SENTINEL in reply`) evaluated on the raw reply before
*blocks end-to-end: True · confidence 0.9*

**Evidence.** autofix.py:135 `if NO_FIX_SENTINEL in reply:` — an unanchored substring test over the whole raw reply, run *before* fence stripping (autofix.py:144) and for *every* strategy, even though only strategies.py:150 (align_expected_value, rung 1 of one ladder) ever tells the model the sentinel exists. orchestrator.py:204-209 turns one such reply into `record.skipped_reason = ...; return record` — no further rung is tried. Verified live: the reply "I considered whether to emit NO_TEST_FIX_NEEDED, but the test is wrong. Here is the fix: def test_x(): ..." tests True.

**Why it matters.** A model that reasons out loud about the rule it was given, or quotes the instruction back, permanently skips a repairable test after one attempt — and that skip is then counted as 'not repairable' and removed from the fix-rate denominator (metrics.py:186-188, 71-80). With a reasoning model at low effort (E1/E4) this kind of echo is common.

**Fix sketch.** Require the sentinel on a line of its own at the start of the reply (the prompt already says 'on its own line'), only honour it for strategies whose prompt introduced it, and treat it as one more rejected rung rather than an abort of the ladder.

**Verifier's correction.** The NO_TEST_FIX_NEEDED opt-out is an unanchored substring test (`autef2/agents/autofix.py:135`, `if NO_FIX_SENTINEL in reply`) evaluated on the raw reply before `strip_code_fences` (autofix.py:144), inside the one code path every ladder rung shares — even though only `align_expected_value`, rung 1 of the ASSERTION_MISMATCH ladder (strategies.py:139-152), ever tells the model the sentinel exists, and even though that prompt asks for it "on its own line". Any reply that merely mentions the token — a model reasoning aloud about the rule, quoting the instruction back, or leaving it in a comment — is read as "the source is at fault": the returned fix is discarded unread, and orchestrator.py:204-209 sets `skipped_reason` and returns immediately, so rungs 2 and 3 (`correct_test_logic`, `rewrite_test_function`) are never tried and cache.record_failure (orchestrator.py:221) is never reached. The skip then lands in `skipped_non_repairable` (metrics.py:186-188) and is removed from the `repairable` denominator behind the headline `fix_rate` (metrics.py:67-80) — though `fix_rate_all` is also reported (metrics.py:83-85, 306-308), so the strict view is available. The mis-detection is unvalidated 

### [major] The weakening guard grades bare pytest `assert` statements on a flat scale: guards.py:85 assigns every `ast.Assert` a specificity of 4 unless `_is_trivial_test`
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.9*

**Evidence.** guards.py:82-87: `if isinstance(node, ast.Assert): profile.total += 1; profile.methods.append("assert"); profile.specificity += 1 if _is_trivial_test(node.test) else 4` — a flat 4 for any non-trivial assert, with no inspection of the comparison operator or operands. The graded `_ASSERT_SPECIFICITY` table (guards.py:31-42) only applies to unittest method calls. Verified live: `assert r == {'a':1,'b':2}` -> `assert r is not None` gives weakened=False, and `assert r == 42` -> `assert len(str(r)) >= 0` (always true) also gives weakened=False, because `_is_trivial_test` (guards.py:212-218) only recognises literal constants and `x == x`.

**Why it matters.** The generation and coverage agents write pytest-style tests, which use bare `assert` almost exclusively, so the graded specificity table barely applies to the code this framework actually produces. Replacing a value assertion with a liveness assertion is the most common LLM repair shortcut and it is invisible.

**Fix sketch.** Score bare asserts by their comparison shape — equality/containment against a literal high, `is not None`/truthiness low, tautologies zero — and flag a drop in that score the same way the unittest table is handled.

**Verifier's correction.** The weakening guard grades bare pytest `assert` statements on a flat scale: guards.py:85 assigns every `ast.Assert` a specificity of 4 unless `_is_trivial_test` fires, and that helper (guards.py:212-218) only recognises a literal `ast.Constant` or a self-comparison (`x == x`). Consequently a repair that keeps the assertion count and body size but replaces a value assertion with a liveness or tautological one is scored as unchanged strength and passes the guard. Verified live: `assert r == {'a':1,'b':2}` -> `assert r is not None`, `assert r == 42` -> `assert len(str(r)) >= 0`, `-> assert isinstance(r, object)`, and `-> assert r` all give weakened=False with specificity 4->4, while the unittest equivalent (`assertEqual` -> `assertIsNotNone`) is correctly flagged (mean strength 5.0 -> 2.0). Note that `assert r` is the exact anti-pattern the generation prompt calls out as "not a test" (agents/generation.py:122-123). ### [major] The repair loop has no cost or wall-clock budget, and its only work-unit cap is off by default. `max_attempts` (config.py:67) is per-test; the outer loop at orc
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.9*

**Evidence.** config.py:67 `max_attempts` is per-test only. orchestrator.py:107-110 iterates every failure with no aggregate cap: `for index, failure in enumerate(failures, start=1): record = self.repair(...)`. `max_tests` exists but defaults to None (orchestrator.py:80, 99-100). `grep -rniE "max_cost|budget|cost_limit|max_total" src/autef2/` finds only context-character budgets (config.py:86-90) and the per-reply token ceiling (config.py:56); nothing bounds spend or time for a run. Each attempt costs at least one model call plus two pytest invocations (autofix.py:166 run_node and autofix.py:256 run_file).

**Why it matters.** E1's appdirs run produced ~25 generated tests; at 3 attempts each that is up to 75 model calls and 150 pytest runs with no ceiling, on a model priced at $20/M output (config.py:32) whose completion tokens include reasoning tokens. E3 already shows a single phase running 19 minutes. A capstone that cannot state a per-run cost or time bound is hard to defend, and a runaway is indistinguishable from a hang.

**Fix sketch.** Add `max_run_cost_usd` and `max_run_seconds` to AutefConfig, check them at the top of the orchestrator's per-failure loop, and record 'stopped: budget exhausted' in the report so partial runs are labelled rather than silently short.

**Verifier's correction.** The repair loop has no cost or wall-clock budget, and its only work-unit cap is off by default. `max_attempts` (config.py:67) is per-test; the outer loop at orchestrator.py:107-110 iterates every resolved failure with no spend or time guard. A work-unit cap does exist -- `max_tests` (orchestrator.py:80, 99-100) -- but cli.py:294 declares `--max-tests` with no default, making the repair loop the only phase whose cap is unbounded by default while `--max-modules` (5), `--max-coverage-files` (3), `--max-mutants` (20) and `--max-survivors` (5) all carry defaults at cli.py:314-320. The exposure is larger than a single pass suggests: the loop runs twice per pipeline (pipeline.py:94 and `_repair_again` at pipeline.py:112 / 147-158) with `max_tests` applied per invocation, and each attempt costs several model calls -- diagnosis (orchestrator.py:266), strategy selection (orchestrator.py:175), the autofix call, plus re-diagnosis on a changed failure (orchestrator.py:296) -- alongside two pytest runs (autofix.py run_node and the run_file inside `_check_regressions`). Runs are not infinite: config.py:80-81 bounds each pytest invocation at 900 s / 120 s, so a runaway terminates rather than hangs

### [major] `patcher._find_node` (patcher.py:87-110) falls through to a global unique-name `ast.walk` match whenever the requested class is found-but-lacking the method, or
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.88*

**Evidence.** patcher.py:87-110 `_find_node`: when the named class is found but has no such method, it falls through to a global `ast.walk` unique-name match (patcher.py:98-103) and returns it. Verified live on a scratch file with `class BaseChecks(unittest.TestCase): def test_inherited(self)` and `class TestA(BaseChecks)` / `class TestB(BaseChecks)`: `find_function(path, 'test_inherited', 'TestA')` returned a span at lines 4-5 — BaseChecks' method — while `span.class_name` reports 'TestA' (patcher.py:80). replace_function then rewrites the base method for every subclass. If the base class lives in another module, `matches` is 0, `find_function` returns None, and autofix.py:107-112 rejects every rung with 'function not found'.

**Why it matters.** Shared unittest base classes are common in the projects v2 claims to be portable to. Either outcome is bad: an edit attributed to TestA that silently changes TestB, or a test the repairer can never touch at all with a misleading rejection reason. The per-file regression check (autofix.py:243-257) only covers the nodeid's own file, so a cross-file base-class edit is not checked until the final suite run.

**Fix sketch.** When class_name is given and the class exists but lacks the method, walk the MRO explicitly (and across modules), record the *defining* class and file on the span, and refuse a function-scope patch whose span lies outside failure.test_file.

**Verifier's correction.** `patcher._find_node` (patcher.py:87-110) falls through to a global unique-name `ast.walk` match whenever the requested class is found-but-lacking the method, or does not exist at all — verified live: `find_function(f,'test_inherited','TestA')` returns BaseChecks' method at lines 5-6, and even a nonexistent class name ('TestZZZ') returns it. `find_function` stamps the requested class onto the span (patcher.py:79), so the repair record attributes the edit to TestA while `replace_function` (patcher.py:135-159) rewrites the base method shared by every subclass. Because the walk is confined to `failure.test_file`, a cross-file base-class edit is impossible and same-file siblings ARE re-run by `_check_regressions` (autofix.py:243-257) — the uncovered cases are subclasses of that base defined in other files, and siblings that were already failing at baseline and are therefore outside `baseline_passing` (guards.py:179-193). *blocks end-to-end: True · confidence 0.85*

*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.85*

**Fix sketch.** On a cache hit, still run the (cheap) heuristic classifier and refuse the hit when it lands in NON_REPAIRABLE; record the cached confidence in a separate field rather than overloading diagnosis confidence.
