### [major] No repair rung can *actively* obtain a value it does not already have: repair context is limited to whatever the failing assertion happened to print, and the St
*blocks end-to-end: True · confidence 0.8*

**Evidence.** strategies.py:178-211 — the three mock rungs are fix_patch_target (correct the target from the source's imports), fix_mock_behaviour (configure return_value/side_effect from reading the source), rewrite_test_with_mocks (rewrite the isolation strategy). All three are static-prediction tasks over context.py source text. Rung 3 explicitly forecloses the repair that usually works: 'Keep the behavioural assertion -- including any assertion about how the dependency was called' (strategies.py:206-207). No rung instruments the code to record the real call, and no rung is allowed to drop the mock and exercise the real collaborator. Same pattern in the assertion ladder: strategies.py:139-177 never runs anything to learn the actual value (cf. E5, python-tabulate, where the test must predict exact formatting).

**Why it matters.** This is why E1's three mock rungs all failed: three restatements of the same guess, with more context each time. The missing rung is the one that removes the guesswork — run the code once, capture the observed call args / return value, and rewrite the expectation from that — which is also the honest answer to 'why not just weaken the assertion'.

**Fix sketch.** Add a final observe-then-rewrite rung: execute the unit under test (or the mock with a recording wrapper), put the captured call signature/return value in the prompt as ground truth, and let rung 3 drop an over-specified call assertion when the source shows the dependency is called differently.

**Verifier's correction.** No repair rung can *actively* obtain a value it does not already have: repair context is limited to whatever the failing assertion happened to print, and the Strategy abstraction cannot express "run something first".

The ladders in strategies.py:139-211 (ASSERTION_MISMATCH and MOCK_MISCONFIGURATION) are all source-reading tasks, and Strategy (models.py:152-166) has only four knobs — include_source, include_full_test_file, include_project_tree, include_sibling_tests — every one of which selects a static text block. TestRunner exposes only run_suite / run_node / run_fail_fast / run_file (runner.py:63,91,95,109); there is no snippet-execution or value-capture entry point anywhere in src/autef2/ (grep for probe/instrument/exec finds only coverage_tool._exec and eval/benchmark.py:231). So a rung cannot say "call this function with these args and tell me what it returns", or "run the code with a recording wrapper and report the real call args".

Two corrections to the original wording:

1. "None observes it" is too strong. The pipeline does observe a real run and feed it in: runner.py:160 runs pytest with --tb=long, the longrepr is captured (runner.py:337) and rendered into the TRACEBAC

### [minor] The signature cache's failure accounting is dead in the escalation path, so its documented demotion mechanism never fires. orchestrator.py:222-224 debits `attem
*blocks end-to-end: True · confidence 0.94*

**Evidence.** orchestrator.py:222-224 calls `self.cache.record_failure(record.signature, attempted[-1] if attempted else "")` — the LAST strategy tried, which after escalation is rung 2 or 3, not the cached rung-1 strategy forced at orchestrator.py:172-173. cache.py:113-121 `record_failure` early-returns when `entry.strategy_id != strategy_id`. So the cached entry's `failures` counter is not incremented in the escalation path, `success_rate` never falls below MIN_SUCCESS_RATE (cache.py:33,51-56), and the entry stays `reusable` indefinitely. Worse, if a *different* strategy eventually succeeds, cache.py:99-109 replaces the entry wholesale with `successes=1, failures=0`, erasing the history. cache.py:13-14 claims the opposite: 'if the cached strategy does not verify, the run falls straight back to the full ladder and the entry's score drops.'

**Why it matters.** The self-correcting property that justifies the cache does not exist. Once a wrong entry is written — trivial given the over-broad signature above — every matching failure for the rest of the pinned workspace's life burns its first attempt (one third of the budget) on a strategy known not to work, and since max_attempts equals the ladder length (finding 5) that first attempt is a rung the ladder then cannot reach.

**Fix sketch.** Debit the entry by the strategy that was actually forced (carry the cache entry's strategy id through the loop), debit on every non-accepted attempt of it, and merge rather than replace on a different-strategy success.

**Verifier's correction.** The signature cache's failure accounting is dead in the escalation path, so its documented demotion mechanism never fires. orchestrator.py:222-224 debits `attempted[-1]`, but the cached strategy is forced only as `attempted[0]` (orchestrator.py:171-175); with max_attempts=3 (config.py:67) against mostly 3-rung ladders (strategies.py:80-310) the last attempt is a different strategy, and cache.py:117-119 early-returns on the id mismatch. Two other exits (orchestrator.py:159-165 NON_REPAIRABLE, 204-209 source_defect_claimed) return before the debit entirely. Consequently `failures` is essentially never incremented in production, MIN_SUCCESS_RATE (cache.py:33,51-56) is unreachable, and the docstring at cache.py:13-14 ("the entry's score drops") is false. The cache is not, however, unbounded-wrong: record_success (cache.py:99-109) replaces a mismatched entry with the strategy that actually verified, so a bad entry is evicted as soon as any strategy succeeds on that signature — it persists only for signatures no strategy ever fixes. Nor is a ladder rung normally lost: repair_strategy.py:50-64 picks the next *unattempted* rung, so a forced cached strategy from the same ladder merely reord

### [minor] The id "rewrite_test_function" is used by three different Strategy objects — ASSERTION_MISMATCH rung 3 (strategies.py:165), API_MISUSE rung 3 (strategies.py:266
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.9*

**Evidence.** strategies.py:165 (assertion_mismatch rung 3), strategies.py:266 (api_misuse rung 3) and strategies.py:308 (unknown rung 2) all use the id "rewrite_test_function" with three different instruction texts. Verified: `strategy_by_id('rewrite_test_function')` returns the assertion_mismatch rung-3 variant, because strategies.py:342-347 returns the first match in LADDERS iteration order. Two consequences: (a) orchestrator.py:181 adds the id to `attempted`, so after a re-diagnosis (orchestrator.py:216-220) switches ladders, repair_strategy.py:58-62 skips the new ladder's differently-worded rung as 'already tried'; (b) a cache entry recorded from the api_misuse ladder is restored as the assertion_mismatch prompt at orchestrator.py:237.

**Why it matters.** The ladder's stated invariant (strategies.py:14-18) is that a strategy is never tried twice and escalation means a different approach — but the identity used to enforce that is not unique, so a genuinely different approach can be skipped, and the cache can replay the wrong prompt. It also makes the per-strategy success table in metrics.py:196-203 merge three distinct strategies into one row.

### [minor] `llm_agreed` cannot be used as a concordance statistic for the two-stage diagnosis contribution, for two independent reasons. (a) Anchoring: the diagnosis promp
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.85*

**Evidence.** failure_analysis.py:166-171 appends "A static classifier suggests '{heuristic_cause.value}' (confidence {heuristic_confidence:.2f}). Confirm it or correct it" to the user message. **Why it matters.** Any agreement statistic derived from llm_agreed is inflated by construction — the model is told the answer before being asked. For a thesis that offers two-stage diagnosis as a contribution, 'the model confirmed the heuristic N% of the time' is the natural validation number, and as implemented it measures anchoring, not concordance.

**Fix sketch.** Ask blind (no suggested label), compare afterwards, and keep the current anchored prompt only as a separate tie-breaker pass; report both numbers.

**Verifier's correction.** `llm_agreed` cannot be used as a concordance statistic for the two-stage diagnosis contribution, for two independent reasons. (a) Anchoring: the diagnosis prompt hands the model the heuristic's own label and confidence before asking (src/autef2/agents/failure_analysis.py:162-173), and there is no unanchored call, so agreement measures confirmation bias rather than independent concordance. (b) Definitional: the flag is computed on the *adjudicated* cause, not the model's cause (failure_analysis.py:218), and two code paths force them equal — an unparseable `root_cause` falls back to the heuristic (failure_analysis.py:194-195, with `_parse_cause` at 222-229) and a low-confidence model answer is deliberately overridden by a high-confidence heuristic (failure_analysis.py:200-205) — so a non-answer and an explicit disagreement both record `llm_agreed=True`. Contrary to the original claim, the flag is not actually reported anywhere: it reaches the run JSON via Diagnosis.to_dict (src/autef2/models.py:135, 141-148, 249) but appears in no report, metric or CSV (src/autef2/web/report.py:419ff, src/autef2/web/server.py:696-697, src/autef2/eval/metrics.py:176 and 249-284), and no test pins its 

### [minor] `guards._is_broad_exception` (src/autef2/guards.py:234-239) only detects exception broadening when the exception is a bare `Name`/`Attribute` passed positionall
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.85*

**Evidence.** guards.py:234-239 `_is_broad_exception` iterates `node.args` and compares `_call_name(arg)` against ('Exception','BaseException'); `_call_name` (guards.py:201-209) returns None for an ast.Tuple. Verified live: replacing `pytest.raises(ValueError)` with `pytest.raises((ValueError, Exception))` returns weakened=False. The same function ignores the `expected_exception=` keyword form entirely (only `node.args` is scanned).

**Why it matters.** guards.py:165-166 exists specifically to catch 'an expected exception was broadened to bare Exception', and the two syntaxes an LLM most naturally reaches for when a narrow raises fails both slip past it.

**Fix sketch.** Recurse into ast.Tuple elements and also scan node.keywords for expected_exception/expected_warning.

**Verifier's correction.** `guards._is_broad_exception` (src/autef2/guards.py:234-239) only detects exception broadening when the exception is a bare `Name`/`Attribute` passed positionally. It scans `node.args` and never `node.keywords`, so `pytest.raises(expected_exception=Exception)` is invisible; and it resolves each arg through `_call_name` (guards.py:201-209), which returns None for anything that is not `ast.Name`, `ast.Attribute` or `ast.Call` — so `pytest.raises((ValueError, Exception))`, the list/set forms, and an aliased `Exception` all return broad=False. Because `raises` is not in `_ASSERT_SPECIFICITY` and is handled in its own `elif` at guards.py:107, such a context manager contributes to no other counter, so none of the other weakening signals in `compare()` (guards.py:140-172) fire either: `compare()` returns weakened=False with no reasons. Verified live for the tuple, keyword and list forms; the bare `pytest.raises(Exception)` form is correctly caught. The only consumer, `agents/autofix.py:204-213`, therefore accepts a broadened-exception repair as a verified honest fix and the weakening counters in pipeline.py:250, eval/metrics.py:194 and eval/compare.py:448-451 under-report it. Not a blocker

### [minor] strip_code_fences picks the LONGEST fenced block rather than the code block. patcher.py:213-221: `blocks = re.findall(r"```[a-zA-Z0-9_+-]*\s*\n(.*?)```", cleane
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.82*

Verified live: a reply containing a long ```text traceback block followed by a short ```python corrected function returns the traceback. `normalise_fragment` (patcher.py:196-207) then finds no `def`/`@` line, leaves the text intact, and `_validate_fragment` (patcher.py:177-186) rejects it as 'replacement does not parse' — a wasted rung attributed to the model rather than to the extractor.

**Why it matters.** Reasoning models that show their work before answering are exactly the ones in use (E1/E4). One rung out of three is a third of the repair budget, and the rejection reason misattributes the cause, so the failure looks like a model quality problem in the report.

**Fix sketch.** Prefer blocks tagged python/py, and among candidates prefer the last one that parses as Python containing a def, falling back to longest only if none qualify.

**Verifier's correction.** strip_code_fences picks the LONGEST fenced block rather than the code block. patcher.py:213-221: `blocks = re.findall(r"```[a-zA-Z0-9_+-]*\s*\n(.*?)```", cleaned, re.DOTALL); if blocks: return max(blocks, key=len)` — the language tag is matched but left outside the capture group and discarded, and selection is by character count alone. Two failure modes, both reproduced/derivable from this line: (1) a long non-python block (e.g. a quoted ```text traceback) followed by a short ```python fix returns the traceback; normalise_fragment (patcher.py:196-207) finds no def/@ line and leaves it, and _validate_fragment (patcher.py:175-179) raises "replacement does not parse" — verified live with the real module. (2) a model that quotes the ORIGINAL failing test in python before the corrected one returns the original: it parses, defines the expected name, is written, and the rung dies as "still failing" — a silent no-op repair that language-tag filtering alone would not fix. Either way the paid model call at autofix.py:117-131 is already spent and the strategy is consumed at orchestrator.py:181-187 against config.max_attempts=3, and the recorded reason blames the model. ### [minor] The heuristic classifier cannot produce production_bug or flaky_nondeterminism (failure_analysis.py:76-103, 106-128), so both causes — and the whole FLAKY_NONDE
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.82*

**Evidence.** Neither cause appears in `_MESSAGE_RULES` (failure_analysis.py:76-85) nor `_TYPE_RULES` (failure_analysis.py:88-103), and `heuristic_classify` (failure_analysis.py:106-128) can therefore return neither. failure_analysis.py:151-160 and 179-189 show that when the model is unavailable the heuristic stands — so with no model, no test is ever skipped as a production bug and no flaky ladder (strategies.py:276-296) is ever selected.

**Why it matters.** The module docstring claims 'If the model is unavailable or unsure, the heuristic stands. Diagnosis never becomes a single point of failure' (failure_analysis.py:15-16). For two of the ten causes the model is exactly a single point of failure, including the one with an irreversible consequence. It also means any measurement of how often production_bug is diagnosed is a measurement of one model's disposition with no baseline to compare against.

**Fix sketch.** Add at least weak heuristic signals for both (e.g. same assertion failing identically across independently generated tests of the same unit -> production_bug candidate; passes on re-run -> flaky), so the fallback path covers the full taxonomy and the model's rate can be compared to something.

**Verifier's correction.** The heuristic classifier cannot produce production_bug or flaky_nondeterminism (failure_analysis.py:76-103, 106-128), so both causes — and the whole FLAKY_NONDETERMINISM ladder at strategies.py:276-296 — depend wholly on the model, contradicting the docstring's "the heuristic stands ... never a single point of failure" (failure_analysis.py:15-16) for 2 of the 10 causes. The consequential defect is measurement, not safety: `llm_agreed` (failure_analysis.py:218) is structurally always False for these two causes, so any reported diagnosis-agreement rate is biased against them and the production_bug rate has no non-model baseline — directly relevant to HOD criticism E7. The auditor's safety framing is inverted: a production_bug diagnosis causes a no-op skip (orchestrator.py:159-165), the most conservative and fully reversible outcome, and with no model AutoFixAgent rejects every repair anyway (autofix.py:115-116), so nothing is edited. A second model-side route to the same skip also exists via NO_FIX_SENTINEL (autofix.py:135-141, orchestrator.py:204-209).

### [major] llm.py's budget-growth retry fires only when the model returns no content. src/autef2/llm.py:152-154 returns any non-empty choice.message.content before the `fi
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.95*

**Evidence.** src/autef2/llm.py:151-176. `text = (choice.message.content or "").strip()` then `if text: return text` (llm.py:153-154) runs BEFORE the `finish_reason == "length"` check (llm.py:161). So the ×4 growth only rescues the all-reasoning-no-output case. A reply cut off mid-function (finish_reason='length' with partial content) is returned verbatim. Generation caps itself at the same 2048 default: `max_tokens=self.config.max_output_tokens` (src/autef2/agents/generation.py:172) with `max_output_tokens: int = 2048` (src/autef2/config.py:53). The partial file then dies in `validate_generated` as `f"generated code does not parse: {exc}"` (src/autef2/agents/generation.py:265-267). `grep -rn finish_reason src/autef2/` returns exactly one hit, llm.py:161 — nothing else in the codebase ever asks whether a completion was truncated.

**Why it matters.** A full pytest module for a large source file is routinely >2048 tokens. For those modules stage 4 reports a syntax error instead of retrying with a bigger budget, so the module is silently left untested and the portability claim quietly degrades with module size. It also mis-describes the cause to the user: the model answered, the budget was too small.

**Fix sketch.** Check finish_reason before returning: treat 'length' as starved whether or not partial text came back, grow the budget and retry, and only return partial text once the ceiling is reached (then label it 'truncated', not 'does not parse').

**Verifier's correction.** llm.py's budget-growth retry fires only when the model returns no content. src/autef2/llm.py:152-154 returns any non-empty choice.message.content before the `finish_reason == "length"` check at src/autef2/llm.py:161, so a reply truncated mid-file is passed back verbatim; `grep -rn finish_reason src/autef2/` hits only llm.py:161, so nothing downstream detects truncation either. The cap is the 2048-token max_output_tokens default (src/autef2/config.py:53), reaching every code-writing agent: generation passes it explicitly (src/autef2/agents/generation.py:172), while coverage_improve (coverage_improve.py:148), mutation_kill (mutation_kill.py:125) and autofix (autofix.py:123) inherit it from src/autef2/llm.py:112 — so stages 4, 7 and 8 are all affected, not stage 4 alone. Truncated output has two outcomes, both wrong: usually it fails validate_generated as "generated code does not parse" (generation.py:265-267), which misreports a budget shortfall as a model error and drops the module with no retry (enhance.py:145-150); but when the cut lands just after a complete test function, it parses, has a test* function and imports the module, so validate_generated (generation.py:254-293) passes

*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.93*

### [major] The OpenAI client is constructed with no transport budget — `kwargs = {"api_key": ...}` plus optional `base_url`, then `return OpenAI(**kwargs)` (src/autef2/llm
*blocks end-to-end: True · confidence 0.9*

**Evidence.** `kwargs: Dict[str, Any] = {"api_key": self.config.api_key}` plus optional base_url, then `return OpenAI(**kwargs)` (src/autef2/llm.py:88-91) — no `timeout=`, no `max_retries=`. The call site `self._client.chat.completions.create(**kwargs)` (llm.py:122) passes no per-request timeout either. AutefConfig has `suite_timeout_s`, `single_test_timeout_s`, `install_timeout_s` (src/autef2/config.py:80-82) but no LLM timeout at all. Meanwhile `complete` retries 3 times (llm.py:106, 117) and `complete_json` wraps that in another 3 (llm.py:186, 192-193).

**Why it matters.** The SDK default is a 600 s timeout with its own 2 internal retries, so one `complete()` can occupy ~30 minutes and one `complete_json()` ~90 minutes before raising, with no log line in between. On a long pipeline that is indistinguishable from a hang, and the web worker thread (server.py:538) has no way to be cancelled — a run can stall past stage 9 forever. Same class of unbudgeted stall as E3's 19-minute mutation run.

**Fix sketch.** Add `llm_timeout_s` to AutefConfig, pass it as `OpenAI(timeout=..., max_retries=0)` so the app owns the retry budget outright, and log each attempt.

**Verifier's correction.** The OpenAI client is constructed with no transport budget — `kwargs = {"api_key": ...}` plus optional `base_url`, then `return OpenAI(**kwargs)` (src/autef2/llm.py:88-91) — and the call site `self._client.chat.completions.create(**kwargs)` (llm.py:122) passes no per-request timeout either; `_request_kwargs` (llm.py:214-245) builds only model/messages/temperature/max_tokens/reasoning_effort/response_format. *blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.97*

### [minor] Permanent 4xx errors are retried as if transient: `except Exception` at src/autef2/llm.py:123 inspects only for parameter-rejection quirks (llm.py:125) and TLS 
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.88*

There is no `Retry-After` handling, no jitter, and nothing distinguishes a retryable 429/5xx from a permanent 401 (bad key), 404 (unknown model id) or 400 `context_length_exceeded`.

**Why it matters.** A typo'd model or a dead key costs 6 s of sleeps and three identical failing calls per agent invocation across the whole pipeline before anything is reported; a real rate limit gets only ~6 s of backoff, far less than typical Retry-After windows, so a genuinely recoverable 429 is turned into a hard LLMError that fails the stage.

**Fix sketch.** Branch on status: retry 429/500/502/503/504 with Retry-After plus jitter, fail fast on 400/401/403/404 with the provider's message surfaced.

**Verifier's correction.** Permanent 4xx errors are retried as if transient: `except Exception` at src/autef2/llm.py:123 inspects only for parameter-rejection quirks (llm.py:125) and TLS errors (llm.py:132), then applies a fixed `2 ** attempt` sleep (llm.py:140-148) with no status-code check, so a 401 (bad key), 404 (typo'd model id) or 400 `context_length_exceeded` costs 3 identical calls and 6 s of sleep per agent invocation, repeated for every agent call in the run, with no circuit breaker (no such logic anywhere in src/) and no preflight key validation (`pipeline.py:75-81` only checks the key string is non-empty; the `dry_run` return at `pipeline.py:72-73` precedes it). The 429/5xx half of the original claim does not apply: `_build_client` (llm.py:88-91) leaves the OpenAI SDK at `DEFAULT_MAX_RETRIES = 2`, whose `_should_retry` retries only 408/409/429/5xx and whose `_calculate_retry_timeout` honours `Retry-After` up to 120 s with jitter — so rate limits already get status-aware, server-directed, jittered backoff underneath AUTEF's loop. Nor does an LLMError fail a stage: every agent catches it and degrades (failure_analysis.py:179 falls back to the heuristic classifier; autofix.py:129, generation.py:174,

### [minor] Greedy JSON extraction in `_parse_json` (src/autef2/llm.py:373) spans the first `{` to the LAST `}` in the reply, so any model output carrying an extra brace ou
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.88*

**Evidence.** src/autef2/llm.py:366-380. Fence stripping `re.sub(r"^```[a-zA-Z]*\s*|\s*```$", ...)` (line 369) only matches a fence that terminates the string. The fallback `re.search(r"\{.*\}", raw, re.DOTALL)` (line 373) is greedy and runs to the LAST brace anywhere in the reply. Verified by running `_parse_json` directly: `'Here is the answer:\n{"root_cause": "assertion_mismatch", "confidence": 0.9}\nHope that helps {see above}'` returns None, and `'[{"a":1}]'` returns None because line 380 requires a dict. Both are plausible reasoning-model outputs when `response_format` has been dropped via the `no_response_format` quirk (llm.py:243-244).

**Why it matters.** Each miss costs a full extra billed round trip via the complete_json repair loop (llm.py:192-209), and on final failure stage 5 falls back to the heuristic diagnosis (agents/failure_analysis.py:179-189) and stage 6 to the static strategy (agents/repair_strategy.py:107-108) — i.e. the diagnosed-repair contribution quietly degrades to v1-style guessing because of a regex, with only a logger.warning to show for it.

**Fix sketch.** Replace the greedy regex with a brace-balanced scan from the first '{' (and accept a top-level array by taking its first object), and strip fences by locating the first opening fence rather than anchoring to the end of the string.

**Verifier's correction.** Greedy JSON extraction in `_parse_json` (src/autef2/llm.py:373) spans the first `{` to the LAST `}` in the reply, so any model output carrying an extra brace outside the answer object — most likely the schema template echoed back from the prompts at agents/failure_analysis.py:63-65 and agents/repair_strategy.py:37-38 — fails to parse, contradicting the docstring's promise to extract "the first JSON object" (llm.py:189-190). Plain trailing prose without braces parses fine; the array case (`[{"a":1}]` -> None) is correct behaviour, since both call sites (failure_analysis.py:176, repair_strategy.py:106) require object keys and explicitly ask for a single JSON object. The path is only reachable once the `no_response_format` quirk has dropped `response_format={"type":"json_object"}` (llm.py:243-244, 296-299), and one miss costs one extra billed round trip via the corrective retry loop (llm.py:192-209); the fallback to heuristic diagnosis (failure_analysis.py:179-189) or static strategy (repair_strategy.py:107-108) needs three consecutive such replies. The fix is a brace-matching scan rather than a regex, plus a unit test — `_parse_json` currently has none anywhere in tests_autef2/.

The server is threaded with a per-token session dict (server.py:170-171, 978-981, 1074) and `_start` only serialises stages within one session (server.py:527-529), so two tabs — sessionStorage keeps tokens per-tab (app.js:10, 59) — running stages at once interleave Activity panels. ### [minor] The web UI's only clock, `elapsed_s`, is a sum of *completed* stage durations, not a running-stage timer: `_run_stage` accumulates it in its `finally` (src/aute
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.93*

**Evidence.** server.py:504 `session.elapsed += time.time() - started` runs in `_run_stage`'s `finally` — nothing accumulates while the stage is in flight. `_snapshot` publishes that same static value (server.py:641 `"elapsed_s": round(session.elapsed, 1)`) and app.js:727 renders it (`$("elapsed").textContent = state.elapsed_s ? ... : ""`). Measured live: after a completed ingest the field read 9.3 s and stayed put; nothing in the payload carries the current stage's start time.

**Why it matters.** During the 19-minute mutation stage the only clock in the UI shows the time of the *previous* stage. There is no elapsed time, no estimate, and no 'mutant i of n' anywhere except the raw log tail — so a watcher cannot tell a slow stage from a hung one.

**Fix sketch.** Publish `started_at` for the running stage and a per-stage elapsed/ETA (mutants have a known n, so i/n plus mean-per-mutant gives a real estimate); render a ticking counter in the stage card.

**Verifier's correction.** The web UI's only clock, `elapsed_s`, is a sum of *completed* stage durations, not a running-stage timer: `_run_stage` accumulates it in its `finally` (src/autef2/web/server.py:504), `_snapshot` publishes that static value (server.py:641), and app.js:727 renders it unchanged on every 1.5 s poll. No per-stage start time exists on `Session` (server.py:100-124) or in the snapshot payload, so the browser cannot tick a clock itself. During a long stage the field shows the previous stage's total and does not move. Progress is still observable: the Activity card's live log panel (index.html:190-195, fed by `LogCollector` at server.py:88/489 and `session.logs[-120:]` at server.py:646) shows per-unit lines such as stage 8's `[i/n] file:line op -> killed/SURVIVED` (src/autef2/enhance.py:435-440). But those lines carry no timestamp (server.py:88 formats level + message only), so neither the clock nor the log lets a watcher tell how long the current mutant has been running — a slow stage and a hung one look alike. The final report's "Wall clock" (src/autef2/web/report.py:504) is unaffected and correct, since server.py:504 runs in a `finally`.

The key is therefore first validated when `_llm` builds the client (server.py:195-200 -> src/autef2/llm.py:82-87), after stages 1-3 have already cloned the project, built the venv and run the suite; the resulting error is caught into `session.error` (server.py:499-501) and clears the remaining queue (server.py:512-514). The CLI path has the same ordering (src/autef2/pipeline.py:60, :66, :76). ### [minor] An over-size upload is rejected without draining the request body, desynchronising that connection. `_body()` raises before reading when `Content-Length > MAX_U
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.75*

**Evidence.** `_body` raises before reading when `Content-Length > MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` (server.py:858-862) and the upload handler answers 413 and returns (server.py:1017-1021) while `protocol_version = "HTTP/1.1"` keeps the connection alive (server.py:844). The unread body is then parsed as the next request line.

**Why it matters.** Dropping a >200 MB zip does not produce the clean 'That file is too large' the code intends — the connection desynchronises and the page's next poll fails with an opaque error, which app.js swallows (app.js:99 `.catch(function () {})`).

**Fix sketch.** Read and discard the body in bounded chunks (or send `Connection: close`) before replying 413.

**Verifier's correction.** An over-size upload is rejected without draining the request body, desynchronising that connection. `_body()` raises before reading when `Content-Length > MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` (server.py:858-862, limit at server.py:43) and `/api/upload` answers 413 and returns (server.py:1015-1021) without sending `Connection: close`, while `protocol_version = "HTTP/1.1"` (server.py:844) keeps the connection alive, so the stdlib's `handle_one_request` loop parses the undrained zip bytes as the next request line. Because the server is a `ThreadingHTTPServer` (server.py:34, 1074) the damage is confined to the upload's own connection — the `/api/state` poll at app.js:94-100 is unaffected. The user-visible effect is that the upload fetch (app.js:150-156) tends to reject with a generic transport error that surfaces via `failed` at app.js:147, so the intended "That file is too large." message is typically never shown. The same undrained path exists in `_payload()` (server.py:864-868), where an oversize POST to e.g. `/api/source` desyncs identically and returns a misleading 400 instead of 413.

### [major] The artefact asserts deterministic sampling it does not implement, and records nothing that would let a reader detect the difference. `temperature: float = 0.0`

On a gpt-5-family model the first request is rejected and every subsequent request in the process omits temperature, defaulting to 1. ### [major] Mutants the suite could not judge are counted in the mutation-score denominator as misses, and the report's own prose says they are excluded. enhance.py:419-421
*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.9*

**Evidence.** `enhance.py:429-433` sets `mutant.error = "the suite did not run under this mutant"` (the timeout/crash case) and leaves `mutant.killed` False. `models.py:451-467`: `total = len(self.mutants)`, `killed = sum(1 for m in self.mutants if m.killed)`, `score = killed / total`, `survivors() = [m for m in self.mutants if not m.killed]` — none of these filter `m.error`. The serialiser comment at `server.py:612-614` asserts the opposite: "not a kill, and not a gap in the tests either, so it is excluded from the score". It is not excluded.
---
In `MutationPhase.score`, an apply failure sets `mutant.error = str(exc)` and appends the mutant (src/autef2/enhance.py:419-421), and a suite that will not run under the mutant sets `mutant.error = "the suite did not run under this mutant"` (enhance.py:433) while leaving `killed` False (models.py:431). `MutationSnapshot.survived = total - killed` and `score = killed/total` (src/autef2/models.py:458-465) count both in the denominator as misses. The kill agent filters them (`if not m.error`, enhance.py:488) but the reported score does not.

**Why it matters.** Every timed-out or unapplicable mutant silently deflates the reported mutation score and pads the survivor list, so the headline number in the report is not a mutation score at all. With a 960 s per-mutant timeout on a project with any blocking mutant (E3), this is not hypothetical. ### [major] The paired repair comparison is sized without reference to the test it uses, and the fallback it advertises does not exist. The exact two-sided McNemar p-value 
*blocks end-to-end: True · confidence 0.9*

**Evidence.** benchmarks/manifest.json seeds 2+8+8 = 18 faults across three projects. The exact two-sided p-value is `tail = sum(math.comb(n, i) for i in range(k+1)) / (2**n); return min(1.0, 2*tail)` (src/autef2/eval/compare.py:120-125). With every discordant pair favouring v2 (k=0), n=5 gives p = 2/32 = 0.0625 and n=4 gives p = 0.125 — so at most 18 observations, of which most will be concordant, cannot reach p<0.05. No power calculation, minimum-n guard, or warning exists anywhere in eval/.

**Why it matters.** HOD-3's sample-size objection applies to the repair comparison as much as to mutation. A run that can only ever report 'not significant' is not evidence, and the report prints the excuse for it (compare.py:547-550) rather than sizing the sample so the test can resolve.

**Fix sketch.** State the required discordant count (>=6 for one-sided dominance at 5%) in the protocol, raise `inject` per project and add projects until the expected discordant count clears it, and have render_markdown print 'underpowered: N discordant pairs, minimum attainable p = X' instead of a bare p-value.

**Verifier's correction.** The paired repair comparison is sized without reference to the test it uses, and the fallback it advertises does not exist. The exact two-sided McNemar p-value (src/autef2/eval/compare.py:120-125) needs at least 6 discordant pairs all favouring v2 before p<0.05 is attainable (n=5 -> 0.0625, n=6 -> 0.03125, verified by running PairedCounts). benchmarks/manifest.json seeds 18 faults across three projects and caps observations at max_tests 8+10+10 = 28 (manifest.json:29,37,45 -> benchmark.py:262,266), and nowhere in eval/ is that 6-pair minimum stated, checked, or warned about. Worse, the report's own excuse for a non-significant result -- "one repository rarely is, which is what the multi-project benchmark is for" (compare.py:546-549) -- points at an analysis that is not implemented: PairedCounts/p_value exist only in compare.py, which runs one project (compare.py:139-141, 182+), while run_benchmark never pools a McNemar table and writes no p-value (benchmark.py:144-190, 283-306); metrics.py:379-382 only tells the reader to run McNemar themselves on observations.csv. Fixes: pool the paired counts across projects in run_benchmark, and state the minimum discordant count (and the observ

### [major] `render_markdown` (src/autef2/eval/metrics.py:288-387) has no model-availability guard, so `run_benchmark` writes a full zeros table to benchmark.md (src/autef2
*blocks end-to-end: True · confidence 0.9*

**Why it matters.** The multi-project path reproduces exactly the failure the single-project path was built to prevent. A bench run that lost its API quota mid-way produces a tidy benchmark.md showing 0 fixed for both arms, which reads as a measured null result.

**Fix sketch.** Move the `model_calls_succeeded` check (models.py:515-528) into `run_benchmark`, and have render_markdown emit a banner (or refuse the tables) when no arm made a successful call.

**Verifier's correction.** `render_markdown` (src/autef2/eval/metrics.py:288-387) has no model-availability guard, so `run_benchmark` writes a full zeros table to benchmark.md (src/autef2/eval/benchmark.py:290-291) and prints it (src/autef2/cli.py:216) even when every model call failed — the precise artifact compare.py:252-268 and compare.py:400-410 exist to suppress. Agents swallow LLMError individually (agents/autofix.py:129, agents/failure_analysis.py:179, eval/baseline.py:189), so the `except LLMError` abort at benchmark.py:172-175 does not catch a mid-run quota exhaustion. Two aggravations: `run_comparison` itself goes through `run_benchmark` (compare.py:208), so even the guarded single-project path drops an unguarded zeros benchmark.md next to the correctly refusing comparison.md; and when the abort at benchmark.py:172 does fire, `result.skipped` never reaches render_markdown (benchmark.py:101), so benchmark.md reports the surviving projects as if they were the whole declared sample. Not a blocker in the literal sense — the run completes and exits 0 — but the output is an invalid measurement presented as a measured null result.

### [minor] Process-global LLM discovery state (`_QUIRKS`/`_MIN_BUDGET`, src/autef2/llm.py:270-278) makes whichever caller issues the first call of a bench process pay the 
*blocks end-to-end: True · confidence 0.85*

*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.85*

**Verifier's correction.** The `regression` column in observations.csv is structurally always 0 and does not correspond to the report's "Regressions introduced". The column writes `int(record.caused_regression)` (src/autef2/eval/metrics.py:275), which is `fixed and attempts[-1].regressions` (src/autef2/models.py:242-243) — but the autofix agent rejects and rolls back any attempt with regressions while `reject_regressions` is on (src/autef2/agents/autofix.py:216-226), and that defaults to True (src/autef2/config.py:74), so an accepted attempt never carries regressions; and the v1 baseline arm never populates `RepairAttempt.regressions` at all (src/autef2/eval/baseline.py:157). The report's "Regressions introduced" (metrics.py:311) instead comes from `_suite_regressions` (metrics.py:204-207, 225-231), a whole-suite before/after diff covering all pipeline stages, and additionally the per-attempt check is only file-scoped (agents/autofix.py:243-257). Both files are written by the same bench run (src/autef2/eval/benchmark.py:101, 310) with no glossary distinguishing the two, so a reader summing the CSV column gets 0 and may report it as "no regressions in either arm".

*blocks end-to-end: False · confidence 0.94*

A reasoning model is billed for the tokens it spends thinking, so a runaway budget is a runaway bill") and config.py:59-62 `reasoning_effort: Optional[str] = None`. `AUTEF_REASONING_EFFORT` is likewise recognised by from_env (config.py:135-136) but absent from the doc's env-var list.

**Fix sketch.** Add both rows plus the AUTEF_REASONING_EFFORT env var, and note that max_output_tokens is a starting budget that grows x4 up to the ceiling (llm.py:162).

**Verifier's correction.** The documentation never mentions `max_token_ceiling` (config.py:54-56) or `reasoning_effort` (config.py:59-63) anywhere — not just in the AutefConfig table at AUTEF_V2_REFERENCE.md:403-424. A repo-wide grep over all five markdown files returns zero hits for either name or for AUTEF_REASONING_EFFORT. The table is otherwise a complete field-for-field mirror of the dataclass, so the two omissions read as oversights rather than abridgement. The env-var half should be restated: the doc has no env-var list at all (no markdown file mentions AUTEF_MODEL, AUTEF_WORKSPACE, AUTEF_MAX_ATTEMPTS or AUTEF_USE_VENV either), so AUTEF_REASONING_EFFORT is not omitted from a list — the list does not exist; from_env's recognised variables are documented only in the docstring at config.py:133-137. The code default is the opposite: src/autef2/config.py:35 `DEFAULT_MODEL = "gpt-4o-mini"`, echoed by the CLI (`--model  OpenAI model id (default gpt-4o-mini)`, observed from `autef2.cli --help`). A full comparison run on a small repository costs a fraction of a cent." Pricing in config.py:25 vs config.py:32 makes gpt-5.6-sol ~27x the input and ~33x the output price of gpt-4o-mini.

**Why it matters.** A user who clicks 'Run all stages' without opening Run settings is billed at 30x the rate the install guide promised, and the 'fraction of a cent' sentence becomes false. It also breaks the stated experimental control ('held constant when measuring, so a v1-vs-v2 difference is never just one used a better model') because the UI arm and the CLI arm silently use different models.

**Fix sketch.** Pick one default and make it the single source of truth — either mark the gpt-4o-mini option `selected` in index.html or move DEFAULT_MODEL to gpt-5.6-sol — then have /api/config report the effective default and update the three doc tables and the cost sentence from it.

That default contradicts src/autef2/config.py:35 (`DEFAULT_MODEL = "gpt-4o-mini"`), the CLI default, and the docs (AUTEF_V2_EXPLAINED.md:327, AUTEF_V2_REFERENCE.md:406 and :624, INSTALL.md:93), and it overrides the AUTEF_MODEL env var because server.py:187 passes it as an explicit keyword into from_env (config.py:153). Cost impact from config.py:25 vs :30 is 8.3x input / 16.7x output, not 27x/33x. The reasoning_effort default also diverges (web "medium" vs config.py:63 None), so the "held constant when measuring" claim at AUTEF_V2_EXPLAINED.md:327 does not hold across the two entry points.

## critic — additional gaps (10)

### [blocker] Benchmark projects are pinned to no revision and no run records one
**Evidence.** benchmarks/manifest.json:24,35,43 give bare repo URLs ("https://github.com/ActiveState/appdirs", astanin/python-tabulate, tkem/cachetools) with no ref. ingest.py:259-265: with no /tree/<ref> the URL becomes `https://codeload.github.com/{owner}/{repo}/zip/HEAD` — whatever the default branch holds at download time. `_download` (ingest.py:281-320) discards every response header; no etag, no Last-Modified, no checksum is kept. `ProjectLayout` (models.py:314-330) has fields name/root/import_roots/test_roots/source_roots/test_files/dependency_files/declared_dependencies/installable/layout_style/notes — no source URL, ref, commit or archive hash — and `to_dict` is a bare `dataclasses.asdict` (models.py:329-330). `_write_outputs` (eval/benchmark.py:290-311) writes metrics/skipped/duration_s/faults/reports and no project revision. Grep for commit|sha|etag|revision over ingest.py returns only the `/tree/<ref>` regex (ingest.py:216-218).

**Why.** The thesis's entire evaluation sample is three moving targets. Re-running `autef2 bench benchmarks/manifest.json` after the model change (E4/d457fa23) re-downloads whatever HEAD is that day, so a changed number cannot be attributed to the model, to AUTEF, or to upstream having edited its own tests — and no artefact records which tree produced the table already in the thesis. This is the missing half of HOD-2 and E7: run provenance findings so far cover model/seed/config, but the *subject under measurement* is equally unpinned, and upstream can also rename or delete a repo (exactly what happened to this project's own remote, E8), making the numbers unreproducible rather than merely uncomparable.

**Fix sketch.** Accept and record a ref per manifest entry (`"ref": "v1.4.4"`, which ingest.py:259-261 already supports via /tree/<ref>), and store the resolved ref plus the archive SHA-256 on ProjectLayout so it reaches RunReport.to_dict and benchmark.json.

### [major] Only the web server can render the HTML report; CLI and bench cannot
**Evidence.** `render_html` (web/report.py:268) and `build_zip` (web/report.py:521) have exactly two call sites in the whole tree, both inside the HTTP handler: web/server.py:891 (`report.build_zip(session)`) and web/server.py:899 (`report.render_html(report.build(session))`). `report.build(session)` (web/report.py:32-37) takes a live `Session` and derives its payload from `server._snapshot`, so it cannot be fed a `RunReport`. The CLI's only artefact is `--json` (cli.py:280, written at cli.py:400-405); `autef2 bench` writes benchmark.md/benchmark.json/observations.csv (eval/benchmark.py:290-307) and `autef2 compare` writes comparison.md — none of them HTML.

**Why.** The artefact the examiner sees in the live demo (the nine-stage HTML report, with generated code, per-mutant table and repair ladder) and the artefacts the thesis quotes (benchmark.md, observations.csv, run JSON) are produced by disjoint code paths with disjoint data shapes. There is no way to obtain the demo-quality report for any benchmarked project without re-running that project through the browser at full model cost, and no way to obtain the benchmark's paired statistics for the project shown in the demo. 'The pipeline finished' and 'the pipeline produced something an examiner would accept' are literally two different programs here.

**Fix sketch.** Refactor `report.build` to take the neutral payload dict rather than a Session, give `RunReport` an adapter that produces the same shape, and add `autef2 run --html <path>`; the renderer itself needs no change.

### [major] Every run silently neutralises the project's own pytest addopts; the documented escape hatch does not exist
**Evidence.** runner.py:48 `strip_addopts: bool = True`, applied at runner.py:164-165 as `args += ["-o", "addopts="]` inside `pytest_arguments`, which runner.py:150-155 states is shared by the suite run and the coverage run. The comment at runner.py:53-56 says "--keep-addopts restores them" — but `grep -rn 'keep.addopts|keep_addopts|strip_addopts' src/ tests_autef2/ *.md` returns only runner.py:48 and runner.py:164: no CLI flag (cli.py:265-321), no web setting (web/server.py:103-113), no config field, no test, and no caller anywhere passes `strip_addopts=False`. Nothing discloses the override either: grep for `addopts` over web/report.py, web/server.py, web/static/app.js and cli.py returns nothing, and unlike the `python_files` widening — which at least appends a `layout.notes` line (ingest.py:424-427) — no note is recorded.

**Why.** The suite AUTEF measures is not the suite the project runs. A project whose addopts carry `--doctest-modules` loses all its doctests from both the before and after counts; one carrying `--strict-markers`, `-p no:randomly`, `--cov-fail-under` or `-n auto` is silently run under different semantics. Both the stage-3 baseline the report labels "the suite as it arrived" (web/report.py:394) and the stage-9 delta are therefore measurements of a suite the maintainers never run, and the report never says so. It also undercuts the portability contribution: the reason for stripping is sound, but an unannounced, unoverridable change to the measured object is not a defensible experimental protocol.

**Fix sketch.** Add the `--keep-addopts` flag the comment promises (and a web toggle), and always append a `layout.notes` entry naming the addopts that were neutralised so the report can render it.

### [major] The operator's whole environment, API key included, is exported into an uploaded project's test subprocess
**Evidence.** `TestRunner._build_env` (runner.py:242-256) begins `env = os.environ.copy()` and adds only AUTEF_REPORT_PATH, PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE, PYTHONHASHSEED and PYTHONPATH — nothing is removed or filtered. That dict is passed verbatim to `subprocess.run(command, cwd=self.layout.root, env=env, ...)` at runner.py:183-199, and reused for the coverage run via `self.runner.subprocess_env()` (coverage_tool.py:219). The key it carries is real: `resolve_api_key` (config.py:188-217) reads OPENAI_API_KEY from the environment or loads it from `.env` into `os.environ` via `load_dotenv_if_present()` (config.py:200-202), so by the time any suite runs the key is in `os.environ`. The shipped web default `use_venv: False` (web/server.py:107, config.py:79) makes `prepare_environment` return `Environment(python=sys.executable, isolated=False)` (venv_manager.py:64-75), so the uploaded project's tests execute under AUTEF's own interpreter. The stated threat model (web/server.py:45-49) covers the signed-in user, not the uploaded project's author.

**Why.** AUTEF's headline portability claim is 'upload any project and we run its suite'. Executing a third-party repository's test code — at an examiner demo, from a GitHub URL the examiner names — with the operator's OPENAI_API_KEY and every other secret in the environment means a single `os.environ['OPENAI_API_KEY']` in any collected test file (or in a conftest, which runs at import) exfiltrates a billable credential. The same exposure applies to model-authored generated tests, which are written and executed with no human review (agents/generation.py:325-330).

**Fix sketch.** Build the child environment from an allowlist (PATH, SYSTEMROOT, TEMP, PYTHON*, the four AUTEF vars) instead of `os.environ.copy()`, and explicitly drop OPENAI_API_KEY and any *_TOKEN/*_KEY/*_SECRET before spawning pytest.

### [major] Stage 1 has no TLS-interception diagnosis, though stage 4-8 does, and the documented environment breaks it
**Evidence.** `llm.py:132-139` detects an SSL failure via `_is_ssl_error` (llm.py:383-388) and raises a full remedy: "TLS handshake failed talking to the OpenAI API ... disable HTTPS scanning, or point SSL_CERT_FILE / REQUESTS_CA_BUNDLE at the interceptor's root certificate." `ingest._download` (ingest.py:281-320) — the first network call of the demo — catches only `urllib.error.HTTPError` (ingest.py:310-317, a 404-branch hint) and `urllib.error.URLError` (ingest.py:318-319, `f"Download failed: {exc.reason}"`). A TLS-interception failure arrives as `URLError(SSLCertVerificationError)`, so the user gets "Download failed: [SSL: CERTIFICATE_VERIFY_FAILED] ..." with no remedy. `_clone` (ingest.py:325-350) likewise only forwards git's own stderr. Grep for SSL|certifi|CERT|ca_bundle|proxy over src/autef2 hits llm.py only. The project's own docs say this machine class is in scope: AUTEF_V2_REFERENCE.md:626-628, "Antivirus TLS interception breaks pip itself ... CERTIFICATE_VERIFY_FAILED on any install" (matching E6, Kaspersky).

**Why.** On the documented demo laptop the very first stage of the very first demo fails with an unexplained error, and per the confirmed `_worker` finding a stage-1 StageError clears the queue. Worse for the thesis: the documented reproduction command `python -m autef2 bench benchmarks/manifest.json` (AUTEF_V2_EXPLAINED.md:514, INSTALL.md:152) fetches all three projects over HTTPS from codeload.github.com, so the benchmark cannot be re-run at all on that machine, and no local/offline manifest is shipped (all three sources in benchmarks/manifest.json are URLs) nor documented as an option.

**Fix sketch.** Reuse `llm._is_ssl_error` in `_download` and `_clone` to raise the same SSL_CERT_FILE remedy, and ship a manifest variant whose sources are local paths or bundled archives for offline/intercepted machines.

### [major] With the shipped venv default off, stage 2 is a no-op and any project with third-party imports dies at stage 3 with no report
**Evidence.** Web default `"use_venv": False` (web/server.py:107) and `use_venv: bool = False` (config.py:79). `prepare_environment` then returns the host interpreter and installs nothing (venv_manager.py:64-75) — no project dependencies are ever fetched. Verified live: a project whose `tests/conftest.py` imports an uninstalled module gives `ran=False, rc=4, reported=0, failures=0, collection_errors=0` (probe run through `analyse` + `prepare_environment` + `TestRunner.run_suite` with this repo's venv). `_collect_suite` (web/server.py:216-228) turns exactly that into `raise StageError("The test suite could not be executed, so this project is out of scope for repair. " + tail)` — a message that never mentions the venv switch, which is the one control that fixes it (index.html:144-150, "Needed for any project with third-party imports"). Meanwhile the stage-2 chip note reads "venv + pinned pytest" (web/static/app.js:214) and, per `_run_stage`'s unconditional `session.done[stage] = True` (web/server.py:496), gets a green tick for having done nothing.

**Why.** This is the most likely way the live demo dies, and it dies in the least recoverable place: stage 3, before any model call, with the queue cleared and — because the download buttons render only inside `if (state.after)` (web/static/app.js:578, 596-599) — no report at all. The CLI has a working preflight (`autef2 check` prints the full conftest ImportError, verified), but the web UI has no equivalent and the abort text points the examiner at 'out of scope for repair' rather than at the checkbox.

**Fix sketch.** Default `use_venv` on for the web path (or detect declared dependencies at stage 1 and refuse to proceed with a message naming the switch), and make the stage-3 abort text name the venv option; mark stage 2 'skipped' rather than done when it installed nothing.

### [major] The downloaded project.zip silently omits any file it cannot read, including Windows long paths
**Evidence.** `build_zip` (web/report.py:521-544) walks `sorted(root.rglob("*"))` (report.py:535) and writes each file at report.py:541 inside `try: ... except OSError: continue` (report.py:542-543) — no logging, no counter, no note in the report. The paths handed to `archive.write` are plain `Path` objects with none of the extended-length handling ingest applies everywhere else: `_native` (ingest.py:173-195, documented at ingest.py:175-186 precisely because "real projects hit this easily") is used at ingest.py:107, 109, 126, 137, 139, 141, 153, 156 and nowhere outside ingest.py. `patcher.copy_tree` (patcher.py:269-280), the benchmark's arm-reset snapshot called at eval/benchmark.py:251, 256, 286, has the same omission — bare `shutil.rmtree` (patcher.py:273) and `shutil.copytree` (patcher.py:274) with no `_native` and no error handling at all.

**Why.** The zip is the examiner's take-home deliverable: 'here is the project as AUTEF left it'. A project with deeply nested fixtures (the exact django-crispy-forms case ingest.py:177-180 cites) silently loses files from that deliverable with nothing in the report saying so, so a suite that looks complete is not the suite that was run. On the benchmark side the same gap is louder: an OSError in `copy_tree` is uncaught and aborts the whole bench run between arms, which is also the one place where a half-restored working copy would corrupt the comparison.

**Fix sketch.** Route both through `ingest._native` on Windows, and replace the bare `except OSError: continue` with a collected list of skipped paths rendered as a warning in the report.

### [minor] Benchmark artefacts default to an unnamed temp directory that no artefact names
**Evidence.** `bench.add_argument("--output", ...)` has no default (cli.py:348), so `run_benchmark` falls back to `output_dir = Path(output_dir or config.reports_dir)` (eval/benchmark.py:154), and `reports_dir` derives from `workspace` (config.py:112-113) whose default is `tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="autef2_")` — a fresh directory per process (config.py:44-48). benchmark.md, benchmark.json and observations.csv are written there (eval/benchmark.py:290-307). The path survives only as a printed line, `print(f"\nArtefacts written to {result.output_dir}")` (cli.py:217), and as the `output_dir` field inside benchmark.json itself (eval/benchmark.py:95). `src/autef2/README.md:97` is the only place that models the safe habit (`--output results/`); AUTEF_V2_EXPLAINED.md:514 and INSTALL.md:152 both give the bare command with no --output.

**Why.** The files a thesis results chapter is built from land in `%TEMP%\autef2_<random>\reports` by default, subject to Windows temp cleanup, with no copy in the repo and no link from any other artefact. Combined with the confirmed 'no run provenance' finding, a student with two benchmark.md files and no --output has no way to tell which run either came from, or to find the CSV that the same report tells them to run McNemar's on (eval/metrics.py:379-382).

**Fix sketch.** Default `--output` to `./results/<UTC timestamp>/` under the current directory, and have the two doc snippets pass --output.

### [minor] No end-to-end test covers the demo path: run-all, the stage queue, or the download endpoints
**Evidence.** `grep -rn 'run-all|run_all|_worker|report.html|project.zip|report.json' tests_autef2/` returns nothing. The only web-report coverage is two direct calls on a hand-built session: `report.render_html(data)` at tests_autef2/test_portability_and_ui.py:1093 and `report.build_zip(s)` at :1101-1107 (asserting only that the two member names are present). The web tests that exist stop at single-stage behaviour: `test_web_ui_runs_stages_one_to_three_without_a_model` (:939), `test_web_ui_gates_every_stage_until_its_input_exists` (:927), `test_web_ui_serves_generated_files_only_from_the_project` (:1024). Nothing exercises `_worker`'s queue drain and error-clear (web/server.py:507-529), `/api/run-all` (web/server.py:1052-1053), `_download` (web/server.py:884-908) or the stage-1-only gate at web/server.py:941-948.

**Why.** The 152-test suite is cited (AUTEF_V2_REFERENCE.md:639, INSTALL.md:102) as the evidence that v2 is sound, but it pins none of the sequence the examiner will actually watch. Every confirmed defect in the demo path — the queue clearing on a stage error, the download buttons existing only under `state.after`, stage 2 being green while doing nothing, the addopts strip, the stage-3 hard abort — sits in exactly the region with zero coverage, which is also why they accumulated undetected.

**Fix sketch.** Add one test that drives a Session through `_start`/`_worker` for stages 1-9 with a stubbed LLM on the bundled sample project and asserts that all nine are done and that `/api/report.html`, `/api/report.json` and `/api/project.zip` each return a non-empty body.

### [minor] The generated report embeds the absolute workspace path and the signed-in username
**Evidence.** `_snapshot` publishes `"root": str(layout.root)` (web/server.py:665) and the session username (rendered at web/report.py:351, `f' · signed in as {_e(data.get("username"))}'`), and `render_html` prints the working copy as a table row, `("Working copy", layout.get("root"))` at web/report.py:381. With the default workspace that value is a machine-specific temp path — verified live as `C:\Users\FINDM\AppData\Local\Temp\autef2_gcbyt4r4\projects\probe`. The same payload is embedded verbatim in the downloadable JSON (web/server.py:893-896) and in autef2-report.json inside the zip (web/report.py:532-534), together with the session log tail (web/server.py:647), which carries further absolute paths from ingest.py:27 and runner.py's per-run log lines.

**Why.** The report is the artefact handed to an examiner and, in a thesis appendix, published. It carries the author's Windows home directory and account name in the headline subtitle and the environment table. It is also the main reason two reports of the same project on two machines are not byte-comparable, which matters when the thesis wants to show a before/after pair. Contrast `models._SCRUB_PATTERNS` (models.py:106-113), which already strips `[A-Za-z]:[\\/]...` paths for cache signatures — the scrubber exists and is simply not applied to the report.

**Fix sketch.** Render the working copy relative to the workspace root (or as the project name alone), and make the username line optional.


## Refuted

- ~~Mutant samples at different --max-mutants are not nested, so runs are not comparable~~ — The finding's core mechanism is empirically false. src/autef2/mutation.py:102-110 sorts the full population first (`found.sort(key=lambda s: s.key())`, limit-independent) and then draws with a freshly seeded generator (`rng = random.Random(seed); rng.sample(found, limit)`). CPython's random.sample selects sequentially from that fresh stream, so the first k picks of a k-draw equal the first k picks
